- **Slack workspace binding.** `oauth.v2.access` names the workspace the user actually consented on — the authorize URL
  cannot pin it, so the response is the only place that fact exists. The exchange now compares `team.id` (never the
  name) against server-trusted config and refuses **before the store handoff**: a wrong-workspace token never reaches
  the vault. New bounded reasons `workspace_mismatch` / `missing_workspace`.
- **Exact callback allowlist.** `REDIRECT_RE` constrains shape but accepts any host, and the redirect URI is what the
  client secret and authorization code are posted against. Whole URIs are compared as strings rather than parsed for a
  host — `connector-oauth-config.test.ts` asserts this module never contains `.host`, a guard that exists because the
  one mistake this file must never make is trusting a request Host header. That guard caught the first attempt.
- **`oauth-callback-real-runner.ts`** — the one place that decides what a real run trusts. Requires an explicit opt-in,
  refuses in production, and has **no default** for workspace, tenant, connector, correlation, client id or callback;
  an unset workspace is a refusal, not a wildcard. Refusal reasons carry no env value, host or id.
- **Route unchanged, deliberately.** `withSlackClientSecret` needs AWS KMS and `createRunnerAppSecretStore` needs the
  runner's DB identity — in the Vercel request path. That is the boundary crossing docs/81 says needs its own GO.
  Switching the route before those exist would mean a silent synthetic fallback (forbidden) or a route that always
  fails. See docs/81 "Phase 8E".
- **Tests:** 32 new, covering valid flow, expired state, tampered state, replay denial, wrong tenant/connector/
  correlation/redirect/subject, wrong workspace, missing workspace, Slack error, timeout and token-store failure.
  Mutation-tested: removing the workspace check, the callback allowlist, the workspace-required rule, the production
  guard, or the replay gate each fails the suite.
- No Slack contact, no production, no scheduling, no new scope. `npm test` 2175 passed | 22 skipped; `tsc` clean.

### fix(db) — connector execution control plane: deny-all EXECUTE hardening + review fixes (P5E10–P5E12, staging-applied) · 2026-07-13

- **Migrations `0044` + `0045` applied to hosted staging** (`ycdpz…`; production untouched). `0044` is the provider-neutral connector **execution control plane** — 6 Tier-2 deny-all tables + 25 `SECURITY DEFINER` functions gating every connector run S1–S5 (discovery-only / promotion-disabled / one-shot enforced by CHECK). It **activates nothing**: a run still requires an approved+claimed authorization, an acquired fenced lock, and every applicable kill switch enabled.
- **Hosted privilege P0 found + fixed.** Applying `0044` to staging revealed that Supabase's `ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES` grants EXECUTE on new `public` functions to `anon`/`authenticated`, so `0044`'s `revoke … from public` left all 25 functions anon/authenticated-executable via PostgREST RPC (incl. `admin_upsert_kill_switch`). Forward-only **`0045`** revokes EXECUTE from `public, anon, authenticated`; re-verified on staging: request-role EXECUTE = **0**; `connector_runner` holds exactly the intended **17**; `service_role` unchanged (trusted). New **CP9b/CP9c** (ACL-level deny-all + runner boundary) + a **default-privilege regression test** reproduce the Supabase mechanism so a future migration cannot reintroduce it. **New required pattern:** every deny-all / control-plane migration must `revoke execute … from public, anon, authenticated`.
- **Review hardening on `0044`** (three-reviewer pass): `admin_reconcile_stuck_run` crash recovery (refuses a live run), DB-enforced kill switch inside `runner_claim_authorization`, `cra_one_active_per_connector` partial unique index (claim-TOCTOU backstop), lease-seconds validation, tightened alert-URL sanitizer.
- Evidence: [`P5E10_CONTROL_PLANE_REVIEW`](./evidence/P5E10_CONTROL_PLANE_REVIEW.md), [`P5E10_PRIVILEGE_FIX_REVIEW`](./evidence/P5E10_PRIVILEGE_FIX_REVIEW.md). **For this Microsoft Entra execution work, RISK-007 stays OPEN and the Entra live path (Phase C) stays BLOCKED; `microsoft_entra` remains `certificationOnly`; staging only.**
- **S1 run executed (after the fix was reviewed + approved, 2026-07-13):** exactly one durable, control-plane-gated Entra discovery task ran on staging — exit 0, `records_seen=5` (Graph `/v1.0/users`), terminal `succeeded`, fenced lock released, **no promotion** (facts `review_status=pending`; total Entra facts intact at 5 via idempotent re-discovery), ECS idle afterward, kill switch returned to fail-closed. **Gate S1 = PASS.** S2 = disabled dry-run only (**FAIL/disabled**); S3–S5 **BLOCKED**. Evidence in connector-runner `docs/evidence/P5E10_ENTRA_S1_REPEAT_RUN.md` + `P5E11_ENTRA_S2_DRY_RUN.md`.

### fix(risk-007) — KMS/IAM verifier: evidence KMS separation from the key policy, not SimulatePrincipalPolicy · 2026-07-03
- **Why:** the second mode-B hosted rerun (main `96270e1`) passed web/secret/alias but errored every KMS row with `Invalid caller — Caller is not present and cannot be implied from policySourceArn`. Root cause: `iam:SimulatePrincipalPolicy` **cannot** evaluate a **role** principal against a KMS key policy passed via `--resource-policy` — resource-policy evaluation needs a concrete caller a role ARN can't imply, and KMS authorization isn't modeled by the IAM simulator. (This is a second, distinct `SimulatePrincipalPolicy`+key-policy limitation after #238's `Resource:"*"` one — so the simulate-with-resource-policy approach is abandoned for KMS.)
- **Fix (Option B — structural key-policy analysis):** KMS rows are now decided from the CMK **key policy** (`kms:GetKeyPolicy` metadata — no decrypt) via pure `evaluateKeyPolicyAccess` / `kmsAccessFromKeyPolicy`: *explicit Deny > explicit Allow > implicit deny*, matching Principal (exact role ARN) + Action (`kms:*`). Verdicts: **allowed** (task role explicitly granted), **denied** (no grant), **overbroad** (allowed only via a `*`/account-root principal — not "allowed", and it also flips the denied rows), **error** (unparseable/absent → fail-closed, never a silent pass). `principalMatch`/`actionMatch` are the pure building blocks. Removed the now-dead `--resource-policy`/`scopeKeyPolicyToResource` path; `buildSimulateArgs` (Secrets-Manager only, no `--resource-policy`, never `--resource-arns "*"`) stays.
- **No weakening:** task→vault-CMK allowed, task→decoy denied, exec→KMS denied are all proven from the key policy; web stays `no_web_aws_principal`; Secrets-Manager rows still simulated + passing; alias check unchanged. A wildcard/root KMS grant now fails as `overbroad`; a key-policy fetch/parse failure fails closed as `error`.
- **Adversarial review closed 3 fail-OPEN gaps in the analyzer:** (1) a bare 12-digit account-id principal (`{"AWS":"123456789012"}`) is now flagged `overbroad` like `…:root` (AWS treats them identically); (2) `actionMatch` is a case-insensitive glob so partial wildcards AWS honors (`kms:Decrypt*`, `kms:*Decrypt`) match — under-matching a grant was fail-open; (3) a statement with `NotPrincipal`/`NotAction`/`Condition` is non-analyzable → `error` (fail-closed) instead of being silently skipped, which could have hidden a broad `Allow` on a DENIED row.
- **Live path uses only 3 read-only verbs:** `kms:GetKeyPolicy` (KMS structural), `iam:SimulatePrincipalPolicy` (Secrets Manager), `kms:ListAliases` (alias). No `--resource-policy`, no kms decrypt, no generateDataKey, no get-secret-value, no ECS.
- **Tests:** selftest 11 checks (structural key-policy + the fail-open regressions); **21 verifier tests** (task-5 coverage: passes only with the task principal + required actions; fails on missing task / exec-allowed / decoy-allows-task / overbroad `*`/root/bare-account / partial-action-wildcard leak / NotPrincipal·NotAction·Condition→error / explicit Deny / unparseable→error; `principalMatch`/`actionMatch`/`evaluateKeyPolicyAccess` units). Zero AWS calls in the suite.
- **No hosted run this PR:** no AWS call, no `get-secret-value`, no KMS decrypt/generateDataKey, no ECS, no role creation, no production, no secret/DB-URL value. docs/49 rewrites the evaluation model; docs/52 records both rerun failures + the fix, criterion 5 stays **BLOCKED (pending rerun)**. RISK-007 remains **OPEN**; Phase C remains **BLOCKED**.

### fix(risk-007) — KMS/IAM verifier: don't send `*` to SimulatePrincipalPolicy (wildcard-resource bug) · 2026-07-03
- **Why:** the first mode-B hosted rerun (main `5e4950c`) passed the web/secret/alias rows but **errored every KMS row** with `InvalidInput … ResourceNames are not in a valid ARN format: *`. Root cause (KMS vs Secrets-Manager rows differ **only** by `--resource-policy`): the CMK **key policy** — fetched and passed via `--resource-policy` — contains `"Resource": "*"` statements, and `iam:SimulatePrincipalPolicy` rejects the bare `*` as an invalid-ARN ResourceName.
- **Fix (two pure, unit-tested builders in `scripts/verify-connector-vault-kms-iam-separation.mjs`):** `buildSimulateArgs()` **never** sends `*` to `--resource-arns` — a wildcard/absent resource **omits** the flag (the API then defaults `ResourceArns` to `*`, which is valid); a real ARN is passed. `scopeKeyPolicyToResource()` rewrites each key-policy `Resource: "*"` → the **CMK's own ARN** (semantically identical for the key it's attached to) before it reaches `--resource-policy`, so no bare `*` is validated. The identity∪key-policy evaluation and all allowed/denied semantics are unchanged.
- **Preserved:** PR #237's `CONNECTOR_VAULT_WEB_ROLE_ARN=NONE` exact sentinel; web rows not simulated in NONE mode; only `identity==="web"` passes by `no_web_aws_principal`; non-web rows still simulated and can fail. Live path still uses **only** `iam simulate-principal-policy` + `kms get-key-policy` + `kms list-aliases` — no new AWS verb.
- **Tests:** selftest 8→10 (builder omits `--resource-arns *`; scope rewrites `*`→CMK) + 4 unit cases (wildcard/absent → no `--resource-arns *`; real ARN passed; secret row has no resource policy; scope rewrites `*`, keeps specific ARNs, fail-safe on non-JSON). **17 verifier tests pass; zero AWS calls in the suite.**
- **No hosted run this PR:** no AWS call, no `get-secret-value`, no KMS decrypt, no ECS, no role creation, no production, no secret/DB-URL value. docs/49 documents the wildcard handling; docs/52 records the bug + fix, criterion 5 stays **BLOCKED (pending rerun)**. RISK-007 remains **OPEN**; Phase C remains **BLOCKED**.

### chore(risk-007) — KMS/IAM verifier: explicit NO_WEB_AWS_PRINCIPAL mode (Step 2 unblock, no hosted run) · 2026-07-03
- **Why:** the Step 2 hosted KMS/IAM verifier was blocked — the verifier hard-required a `CONNECTOR_VAULT_WEB_ROLE_ARN`, but the v3 web/request runtime has **no AWS principal by design** (Vercel + Supabase RLS; hosted preflight found no web/request role in staging `833822972703`). "No principal" is **stronger** evidence than "a role that's denied": with no AWS identity the web path cannot authenticate to AWS at all.
- **`scripts/verify-connector-vault-kms-iam-separation.mjs`:** accept the **exact** sentinel `CONNECTOR_VAULT_WEB_ROLE_ARN=NONE` → `noWebPrincipal` mode. In it, the web ARN is dropped from `REQUIRED_ENV`, `identity==="web"` rows are recorded `no_web_aws_principal` and pass **by absence** (never simulated — `iam:SimulatePrincipalPolicy` is not called for them), and a live-run banner prints the 3 evidence anchors. **Only exact `NONE`** triggers it — unset/empty → still refuses; a typo (`none`/`None `) or real ARN → simulated (bogus → `error` → FAIL). **All other rows (task/exec/decoy/DB-URL/connector-secret/alias) are evaluated identically** — the sentinel gates on `identity==="web"` only.
- **Tests (selftest 6→8 + 4 unit cases):** NO_WEB passes the web row as `no_web_aws_principal` without simulating it; NO_WEB does **not** weaken non-web rows (an exec-decrypt leak still FAILs); the `NONE` gate drops only the web ARN (a missing connector-secret still refuses, listing it, not the web ARN); a non-`NONE`/unset web ARN stays required. Spawned gate tests refuse **before** any AWS call — the suite makes **zero** AWS calls.
- **docs/49:** documents the two web-evidence modes (A role-simulated / B no-principal) + the 3 anchors (IAM list has no web role; `check-app-runtime-imports.sh` bars KMS/Secrets-Manager on the request path; the request-path token source hard-throws) + the `NONE` operator command. **docs/52:** criterion 5 → **BLOCKED (pending rerun)** with the preflight facts; criterion 11 reframed (no web AWS principal at all); the pending-gates list updated. Still **not a final PASS**.
- **No hosted run this PR:** no AWS call, no role created, no `get-secret-value`, no KMS decrypt, no ECS, no production, no real token/OAuth, no secret/DB-URL value. RISK-007 remains **OPEN**; Phase C remains **BLOCKED**.

### fix(sync) — Slack users.list completeness signal; absence marking only on a complete fetch (#234 ceiling) · 2026-07-03
- **Closes the #234 truncation ceiling.** `slack-client.ts` `listUsers()` now returns an explicit `{ users, complete, reason, usersFetched, pagesFetched }` instead of a bare array: `complete` is true ONLY when pagination reached the natural last page (empty `next_cursor`) without hitting the page cap or a repeated cursor. The SILENT truncation cases (`page_limit_reached`, `cursor_loop`) now report `complete:false`; a hard mid-stream error (http / ratelimit / malformed / Slack `ok:false`) still THROWS `SlackApiError` (fails the whole sync). `reason` is a SAFE class only — never a token / response body / cursor value.
- **Absence/stale marking is gated on completeness.** `applySlackDiscoveryResolution(..., { syncComplete })` runs `markAbsentAppUsersStale` ONLY when `syncComplete` (plus the existing ≥1-user + valid-observed_at guards). On an incomplete fetch, **present users are still upserted** (last_seen/active — non-destructive), but **nothing is marked stale** (an absent-looking user on a truncated fetch is not falsely staled). The orchestrator threads the client's `complete` and logs a **safe reason class** (`console.warn` — never a token/PII) when it skips.
- **Fail-closed union:** partial/truncated → no stale marking (new gate); mid-stream error → whole sync fails (existing throw → resolver never runs); 0-user → no marking (existing resolver guard); a returning complete sync marks normally (proven — the gate is not a permanent skip).
- **Tests:** client completeness (single-page / multi-page complete; zero-user complete; cursor-loop → `cursor_loop`; never-ending cursor → `page_limit_reached` at MAX_PAGES; mid-stream error THROWS) · resolver (`syncComplete:false` skips marking, present users still written, a later complete run then marks) · orchestrator (a looping client skips marking, still writes present users, logs the safe reason, no token leak). Callers updated to the result shape; `next` types unaffected.
- **No live Slack / AWS / KMS / Secrets Manager / OAuth / ECS / production; fixture/synthetic clients only; no real token; no hard deletes.** RISK-007 remains OPEN; Phase C remains BLOCKED.

### feat(sync) — wire non-destructive Slack absence/stale marking (0040 columns) · 2026-07-03
- **Runtime wiring of the 0040 absence fields (no schema change).** The Slack resolver now records presence and, after a COMPLETE successful sync, marks departed users **stale** — never deletes. Present users' upsert sets `last_seen_at = observed_at` + `sync_status='active'` (a returning stale user reactivates); then `store.markAbsentAppUsersStale({tenantId, appId, observedAt})` runs one **UPDATE** flipping this tenant+app's still-`active` rows whose `last_seen_at` predates `observed_at` (or is NULL) to `'stale'` — **UPDATE-only, never a delete; `last_seen_at` kept**.
- **Safety guards:** marking runs **only after every write succeeds** (any store throw aborts before the mark, so a failed/partial sync marks nothing) **and only when ≥1 user was seen** (a 0-user "successful" sync never mass-marks). Tenant+app scoped, member-JWT/RLS (existing 0004 policy); **no service-role**, no new grant.
- **Deleted vs absent:** a Slack `deleted:true` user is still *returned* → **present → stays `active`**, and the resolver now propagates `raw_payload.slack_is_deleted=true` (previously dropped) so it stays distinct from an **absent → `stale`** user.
- **Run summary/audit:** `SlackResolutionSummary.staleMarked` + `RunSlackSyncSummary.staleMarked` carry the count; the recorder persists `manual_sync_runs.app_users_marked_stale`.
- **Files:** `slack-resolver-write.ts` (interface + wiring + summary), `supabase-slack-resolver-store.ts` (`markAbsentAppUsersStale` + `last_seen_at`/`sync_status` on upsert), `run-slack-sync-dev.ts` + `manual-sync-run-recorder.ts` (summary/persist), `slack-sync-fixture.ts` (`excludeUserIds` for the departed-user scenario), + unit + fixture-e2e tests.
- **Tests:** resolver unit (present→active, second-sync-marks-only-absent, idempotent, reactivates, 0-user guard, mid-resolve-failure marks nothing, deleted-stays-active+provenance); store unit (mark_stale is an UPDATE, tenant+app+active+not-seen scoped, safe-code error); recorder persists the count; **fixture e2e on the real stack** (first sync all active + last_seen; later sync marks only the departed user, row kept, count recorded; returning user reactivates; deleted-but-present stays active). No `.delete()` in the runtime sync/resolver path.
- **Known ceiling (ponytail, follow-up):** absence marking trusts `users.list` returned a COMPLETE member list; `slack-client.ts` silently caps at `MAX_PAGES` and breaks on a repeating cursor, so a >20k-member workspace / cursor loop could present a truncated set and mark the tail stale. Non-destructive + reversible (self-heals on the next complete sync); the real fix (listUsers signalling completeness / failing closed on truncation) is deferred. The store also rejects any `observedAt` carrying a PostgREST filter delimiter (defends the `.or()` interpolation).
- **A 2-agent adversarial review passed** (non-destructive correctness + tests/scope/governance); applied its hardening notes.
- **No live Slack / AWS / KMS / Secrets Manager / OAuth / ECS / production; no hard delete-from-absence.** RISK-007 remains OPEN; Phase C remains BLOCKED.

### feat(verifier) — inert-by-default connector-vault KMS/IAM separation matrix verifier (RISK-007 leg) · 2026-07-03
- **New `scripts/verify-connector-vault-kms-iam-separation.mjs`** — an **evaluation engine** (not a runbook emitter) for the hosted KMS/IAM separation boundary. It decides an allowed/denied **matrix** via **`iam:SimulatePrincipalPolicy` + `kms:ListAliases`** — which *evaluate* access and **never perform** it: **no `kms:Decrypt`, no `kms:GenerateDataKey`, no `secretsmanager:GetSecretValue`, no ECS, no DB, no secret value read**. Complements (does not replace) `verify-staging-kms-iam-separation-dry-run.mjs` (the human synthetic-decrypt runbook).
- **Matrix (9 access rows + 1 alias-scope):** task role CAN decrypt/GenerateDataKey the vault CMK + read the connector secret; **web AND execution roles CANNOT decrypt** (load-bearing); the runner grant is **CMK-scoped, not wildcarded** (a *decoy* CMK must be denied for BOTH Decrypt and GenerateDataKey); execution role reads **only** the DB-URL secret (connector secret **denied**); and the CMK **resolves to the expected alias**. For KMS rows the live path evaluates *identity ∪ CMK key policy* (`kms:GetKeyPolicy` → `--resource-policy`), so a direct key-policy grant can't hide. A single failing row fails the run.
- **Inert + fail-closed by default:** refuses unless enabled + confirmed, **rejects the production ref**, requires the staging ref + every identity env var. The agent and CI run **only `selftest`** (mocked IAM/KMS functions, zero AWS); `npm run verify:kms-iam:selftest`. **Live is operator-only** and was **not run**. Output is **verdicts + metadata only** (identity label, action, redacted resource tail, expected/actual/PASS-FAIL) — never a secret, ARN, account id, or key material.
- **Tests (`…test.ts`, in CI via `npm test`):** the matrix logic + failure detection — wildcard KMS resource, web/exec decrypt leak, wrong CMK/alias, exec reading the connector secret, output redaction, and inert/production-refusal guards — **9 tests, mocks only, no real AWS client constructed**. Docs: **`docs/49_KMS_IAM_SEPARATION_VERIFIER.md`** (operator run process, expected output, evidence to record, what does/does not close RISK-007).
- **No AWS / KMS / GetSecretValue / ECS / secret read / hosted run.** A green live run would be hosted evidence for the KMS/IAM separation **only** — audited access/use + rotation/revocation + lifecycle remain. **RISK-007 remains OPEN; Phase C remains BLOCKED.**

### fix(ci,db) — store-it root cause: migration 0039 failed under the non-superuser `supabase start` · 2026-07-03
- **Root cause (not infra).** The store-it CI outage after PR #225 (`No such container: supabase_db_idcaddie-v3`, 3× reruns) was **migration `0039`**, not CI infrastructure. `supabase start` — and any **hosted Supabase apply** — runs migrations as a **non-superuser** role (`supabase_admin`, CREATEROLE), so `0039`'s `alter role connector_runner_login … nosuperuser nocreatedb nocreaterole noreplication nobypassrls` aborted with `42501 permission denied to alter role` (clearing superuser/replication/bypassrls requires a true SUPERUSER). That abort tore down the stack, and the downstream `supabase status` reported the missing container. `test-rls.sh` never caught it because it applies migrations as the superuser `postgres`; the real error was **masked** by `test-store-it.sh` swallowing `supabase start` output (`>/dev/null 2>&1 || true`).
- **`supabase/migrations/0039` — apply cleanly under a non-superuser role.** Dropped the privileged attribute changes; `create role … login noinherit` already yields the secure PG defaults (nosuperuser/nocreatedb/nocreaterole/noreplication/nobypassrls), so only `alter role … login noinherit` remains. **T57 still verifies the full secure shape.** This also unblocks `0039`'s real purpose — reproducible **hosted** provisioning would have failed identically. Edited **in place** (not a forward-fix `0040`): `supabase start` applies `0039` before `0040` and aborts on it, and `0039` was **never applied to any durable/hosted DB**.
- **`scripts/test-store-it.sh` — reliable + diagnosable.** Stop swallowing `supabase start` errors (fail loudly); bring up only the services the IT uses (db/kong/rest/meta/auth), excluding the heavy/flaky ones (analytics/vector/edge-runtime/functions/imgproxy/inbucket/realtime/storage/studio); retry once after a clean stop; on failure print **safe, secret-free** diagnostics (`docker ps -a`, `supabase status`, discovered `supabase_db_*` logs).
- **Validation:** `supabase start` (non-superuser) now applies `0039` + brings up the stack + **store-it 12/12** (was: stack torn down); RLS suite green (T57 holds under superuser); migration-safety PASS; secret scan clean; a focused adversarial security review confirmed no weakening (fresh-create defaults + T57 coverage). **No hosted apply / AWS / ECS / GetSecretValue / secret read.** RISK-007 remains **OPEN**; Phase C remains **BLOCKED**.

Added a **second valid cleanup state** without weakening the first: (State 1) old profile still exists locally → live `sts get-caller-identity` must fail with a revoked-key class (`InvalidClientTokenId`/`UnrecognizedClientException`; **`AccessDenied` still fails closed** — a still-live-but-denied key is never "dead"); (State 2) old profile already removed locally → PASS the **local** cleanup (`local_profiles_removed`, detected via read-only `aws configure list-profiles`), which proves the credentials are gone locally but does **not** claim AWS-side deletion (that stays the operator's authoritative Console action). Old profile names now **default to the pinned identities** (profiles/region no longer hard-required; opt-in/env/staging-ref guards unchanged).
- **Recorded RESULT (docs/47, doc 42 §91.7(f)):** Console deletion **operator-attested**; `aws configure list-profiles` returns only `default` → both old profiles **removed locally**; live-sts probe superseded by the removal → step (f) cleanup **complete**. No key id/secret/ARN recorded.
- **Fail-closed against masking a live credential:** if `aws configure list-profiles` itself fails, the run FAILs (cannot verify) rather than assuming "removed"; and if the operator **explicitly** sets a profile name (`AWS_PROFILE` / `ID_CADDIE_RUNNER_KEYS_REVOKED_WEB_PROFILE`), that profile is always **live-probed** even when absent from the list (a not-found profile yields a non-revoked sts error → fails closed), so an explicitly-named still-usable credential can never be silently reported as `local_profiles_removed`. Only a *defaulted* (unset) name that is absent from the list counts as local cleanup.
- **Guardrails:** still READ-ONLY — the ONLY aws calls are `sts get-caller-identity` + local `aws configure list-profiles` (no network, no secrets); no key/IAM/KMS/Secrets-Manager mutation, no crypto, no Postgres, no production touch, no new dependency, no migration. The vitest **static** test allowlists exactly those two actions (deny-by-default), keeps the revoked-key-class assertion (no `AccessDenied`/`SignatureDoesNotMatch`/`ExpiredToken` false-PASS), asserts the `local_profiles_removed` path exists and does not claim a live check, and keeps the redaction/no-key-material checks. A new vitest **behavioral** test (`check-runner-keys-revoked.behavior.test.ts`) stubs `aws` on PATH (no network) and asserts `run_checks` for all branches: State-2 local-removed, State-1 revoked, AccessDenied→fail-closed, still-works→FAIL, enumeration-failure→FAIL, and explicit-absent→live-probed-fail-closed. Selftest 11 guard cases (no AWS), wired in CI. **No credentials in docs. RISK-001/RISK-007 remain OPEN; Phase C BLOCKED.**

Result: auth.test+users.list ok, single page; PRESENT id/team_id/deleted/is_admin/is_owner/is_primary_owner/is_restricted/is_ultra_restricted/is_bot/tz/updated/profile.{display_name,real_name,title,status_text}. **Reconciled to the live shape:** `has_2fa`/`has_sso` are ABSENT in P0 users.list → removed from required output, **provenance-only when present** (2FA/SSO posture deferred to Enterprise Grid/SCIM); `profile.email` was ABSENT on the (small-sample, 1 non-bot) member → `email` is **optional** in the client (missing-email + present-email both tested), and PR 3 emits person_identity_candidate only when email exists. **PENDING before PR 3:** Sam re-verifies `profile.email` PRESENT on a member with a known email, or escalates as a blocker. **Rerun was still inconclusive** (withEmail 1 but the workspace has 2 bots + 1 non-bot → the email may be a bot's), so `verify-slack-field-paths-dev.mjs` now breaks email presence **by user type** (`botsWithEmail`/`nonBotsWithEmail`/`nonBotsMissingEmail`) and samples a non-bot WITH email for the field-path block (`pickSampledNonBot` returns booleans only — no raw member). **#188 email merge gate: `nonBotsWithEmail >= 1` AND `profile.email` PRESENT on the non-bot sample** — **CLOSED ✅** (final live run after adding a non-bot member with an email: nonBotsWithEmail 1, botsWithEmail 0, sampledNonBotWithEmailFound true, PRESENT profile.email on the non-bot sample; has_2fa/has_sso still ABSENT). Email is per-user optional (client never requires it); PR 3 emits person_identity_candidate only when email exists. The PR-3 email precondition is satisfied; #188 is merge-ready.
- **No Slack API client beyond auth.test/users.list, no live Slack call in CI/tests, no real token, no customer credential, no OAuth/runner/KMS/IAM, no DB/discovery-fact/resolver/UI, no production enablement, no new dependency, no migration/types.** RISK-001/RISK-007 remain **OPEN**.

### PR #181 — B2c-run prep harness (safe client-secret ingestion + staging readiness) · 2026-06-25
- **Category:** RISK-007 — B2c-run **prep** (the turnkey, reviewed, synthetic-tested path to later load the real Slack client secret). Deep-gated (master-credential ingestion surface). NO real Slack client secret, NO real token, NO Slack API call, NO OAuth run, NO production/hosted command, NO RISK-007 closure, **no new dependency**. Tests 686 → **708**; RLS **620 → 631** (T57); types **1955** (0-diff — `connector_runner_login` is an RLS-test role, not a migration); no doc 17 §5 box ticked.
- **Safe ingestion harness core (`client-secret-ingest-harness.ts`, server-only):** `readSecretFromStream(stdin)` (empty/oversize rejected; never logged) + `ingestClientSecret({ plaintext, appEnv:"staging", version }, { keyProvider, kekId, store })` → the existing `saveSlackClientSecret` KMS/envelope boundary (encrypt-immediately; **envelope-only** `connector_app_secrets` row; **redacted** `secret_id`). `assertSafeInvocation` **refuses** `process.env.SLACK_CLIENT_SECRET` and **any argv secret** (positional/unknown-flag) with **generic** errors that never echo the value. **Fail-closed** on missing KMS config / missing store / invalid `app_env` (staging-only) / invalid version; the **catch returns a static reason and NEVER surfaces the caught error** — a forced store error that *carries the sentinel* is swallowed to `ingest_failed` with no leak.
- **Trusted redirect config (`connector-oauth-config.ts`):** `connectorOAuthRedirectUri()` resolves the EXACT staging callback **`https://idcaddie-v3.vercel.app/connectors/oauth/callback`** from server config only (`CONNECTOR_OAUTH_REDIRECT_URI` override or the staging default) — absolute-HTTPS, exact path, **no trailing slash**, **never request-derived** (no Host/X-Forwarded-Host; a source scan asserts it). **The route now uses this — the `app.example.com` placeholder is removed.** Tests prove the configured value byte-matches the served route path.
- **Guarded operator pre-flight (`scripts/b2c-ingest-client-secret.mjs`):** refuses the production ref (staging-only), refuses `SLACK_CLIENT_SECRET` in env, refuses any argv secret, requires `--confirm`, and **emits the procedure without ever reading/holding the secret** (the harness core reads stdin inside the hosted runner). Guard functions unit-tested; `node --check` clean.
- **`connector_runner_login` prerequisite (deliverable 4):** documented as a **manual, staging-only** step (NOT a committed migration — the LOGIN credential is environment-specific and must never be committed), with the exact minimal-privilege DDL (LOGIN **NOINHERIT**, `grant connector_runner` → may `SET ROLE connector_runner` and nothing more) and a verification query. **RLS T57** creates the role per that DDL and proves: not superuser/createrole/createdb/bypassrls, NOINHERIT, **zero direct table grants**, member of exactly `connector_runner`, and behaviorally (via `SET SESSION AUTHORIZATION`) that it has **no ambient access** until `SET ROLE connector_runner`.
- **Tests (synthetic — 22):** a marked `MUSTNOTLEAK-…` client-secret sentinel — harness (stdin→boundary, envelope-only, no print/log/return, argv/env rejection, every fail-closed path, the forced-store-error-carrying-sentinel no-leak), config (exact match, server-config-only, rejects trailing-slash/wrong-path/http/no-host), launcher guards (prod/env/argv refusal, no-echo). Plus RLS T57.
- **docs/45 §11** added: the exact stdin-only ingestion command + warnings, the staging callback URL + trusted-redirect check, the `connector_runner_login` readiness check, the KMS-key readiness check, the post-ingestion DB/scanner/log checks, and the partial-failure cleanup.
- **Posture:** **prep only — no real secret/token/Slack call/OAuth run.** The hosted runner `RunnerConnection` (`connector_runner_login`) + the real KMS provider remain B2c-run-time prerequisites (the harness takes them as injected deps; the script fails closed without them). **B2c-run remains future + explicitly authorized.** **RISK-001 OPEN. RISK-007 OPEN. Cutover BLOCKED. Connector credentials not production-ready.** Updated docs 45 (§11), 42 (§90.3), 09, 00.

## PR #387 — Phase 8C (part 1): the real Slack HTTP client (2026-08-01)

Phase 0 reconfirmation found the precise missing piece for the real callback: `SlackHttpClient` is a **type with no concrete
implementation**. Every other dependency `makeRealOrchestratorDeps` needs — KMS key provider, envelope store, pending consumer,
ingest deps — already exists.

Adds `slack-http-client.ts`, the one file in the vault that touches the network. Host allowlist checked **before** the request is
attempted (rejecting afterwards would already have sent the secret), `redirect: "error"` so a 30x cannot forward the
credential-bearing body, `cache: "no-store"`, and the underlying network error discarded rather than wrapped because fetch
failures embed the URL and callers log errors.

Eight tests, three mutations caught: removing the host allowlist, following redirects, and wrapping the underlying error.

**No Slack contact.** The route still imports the synthetic handler — wiring it is the next step. **Staging only.**

## PR #386 — Phase 8 (part 1): canonical app-account evidence (2026-08-01)

Migration **0076**. Phase 0 traced every Slack stage and found the blocker was not OAuth: the manifest, executor, exchange, vault
and verification are all real and hosted-proven, but the fact sink is in-memory and **there was nowhere for a SaaS account to
land**. No amount of Slack work could reach a product surface.

Adds provider-agnostic app-account evidence: accounts, groups, memberships, identity matches and per-connector capability state.
Not Slack-specific — the manifest already emits generic `app_user_account`/`group` facts, as do the Okta and Entra normalizers.

Slack is **not** an identity provider: no FK to `identity_accounts` in either direction, and its directory capabilities are
`not_applicable`. Matching has exactly two methods and display-name is not one. Bots and service accounts are categorised rather
than counted as people. `plan_dependent` and `permission_dependent` are distinct states so one gated endpoint never marks a
healthy connector broken.

Also fixed: `test-rls.sh` re-broadens every table after migrations to mirror hosted Supabase defaults, which was **masking** the
new revokes — the same hazard the `files` block documents. The five tables are now re-asserted in lockstep.

Nine SQL groups, seven mutations caught. Doc **81** classifies every Slack stage. **Staging only; not applied.**

## PR #385 — Phase 7B: canonical intelligence layer (2026-07-31)

Migration **0075** plus a new pure `src/lib/canonical/` layer. The objective was not a page: it is the model that lets a new
connector light up every compatible surface without touching product code.

**Capability model.** Two orthogonal axes — SUPPORT (has ID Caddie built it?) and STATE (what does this workspace have?) — kept
apart, because collapsing them is what makes an unbuilt capability render as `0`. Nine states, none of which is a number, each
with a customer sentence. Only Okta has any `implemented` capability today, and only the five directory ones; that is written down
rather than guessed.

**Metric lineage.** One owner per metric, machine-readable, with formula, refresh trigger, connector scope, unavailable state and
security boundary. A test walks it, so the documentation cannot drift from the product.

**Application match model** (0075). `directory_applications` and `public.apps` stay separate; matches are recorded judgements with
confidence and provenance, never a name-based join. One accepted match per directory application, deliberately not unique on the
SaaS side — two Okta organizations may share one contract. No matcher exists; the table is RLS-locked with no policy.

Two modelling errors were caught while building: the composite endpoint FK had no unique key to reference, and the original
SaaS-side uniqueness would have broken multi-directory workspaces.

22 canonical tests, seven mutations caught. Docs 79 (canonical layer) and 80 (legacy review). **Staging only.**

## PR #384 — Phase 7A: executive Home (2026-07-31)

No migration. Home becomes an identity-governance overview: context header, identity summary, then a three-column layout of
access posture / risk / connector health, then a bounded attention queue, with SaaS Intelligence kept clearly separate below.

**Cost: 4 parallel reads, up from 3.** `loadAccessOverview` already paid for a counts RPC plus six bounded sweeps AND already
returned the complete findings list, so the risk panel and the attention queue are projections of a result Home was fetching
anyway. A findings-summary RPC would have bought nothing and cost a round trip. The one addition is the connector inventory for
per-connector health.

Every number is `current`; `totalEvidence` never appears as an active count. Severity counts come from the engine's own summary
rather than being re-counted. No composite score, no trend, no percentage — none of those has data behind it.

Absence is never rendered as health: `too_large` withholds the distribution and shows no findings figure, a failed inventory read
says so explicitly, and in all-active mode the rollup reports the WORST connector and names it rather than showing one green tick.

**A real bug found while testing:** a failed inventory read made Home claim "No directory connected". Onboarding now requires
knowing the estate is empty, which the cheap scope read answers independently.

16 derivation tests, 11 Home tests, seven mutations caught. `identity-overview.tsx` deleted. **Staging only.**

## PR #383 — Phase 6: stale-aware counts (2026-07-31)

Migration **0074**. The counts RPC answered one question with a number used for two jobs: the conservative too-large bound, and
the number shown in the too-large fallback. A directory with 6 current groups and 1 retained stale group displayed "7 groups".

Now explicit: `current` (customer-facing), `stale`, `other`, `totalEvidence` (the gate, exclusively), with the invariant
`totalEvidence = current + stale + other`. `other` exists because `sync_status` permits four values and folding the unwritten two
into `stale` would be a silent miscategorisation. The bound is unchanged — it reproduces the same numbers as before.

Six flat keys retained as documented deprecated aliases of `totalEvidence`; both production callers migrated in the same change.

Seven groups against real Postgres, 8 new application tests, seven mutations caught — including displaying total evidence,
gating on current, deduplicating across connectors, and re-pointing a deprecated key. **Staging only.**

## PR #382 — Phase 5B: unified connector marketplace, instances and lifecycle actions (2026-07-31)

No migration. **Root cause:** `/connectors` asked `getOktaConnectorStatus()`, which reads `okta_connector_configs` — a table only
Okta has. Slack and Entra had real connector rows and no config row, so their cards fell through to the static catalogue's
"Preview / Connection coming soon" while the workspace was looking at those very instances on `/connectors/manage`. The override
was also keyed one-per-provider, so a second Okta organization could not be represented at all.

The marketplace now reads the provider-agnostic `product_connector_inventory`. Provider availability (about the product) and
instance lifecycle (about this workspace) are rendered as two separate facts on one card, so "Preview" and "Configuration saved"
coexist without contradiction — the synthetic Entra connector is exactly that case. Every instance is listed; none is collapsed
into a single badge.

The status filter is **Configured**, not Connected: a saved configuration is not a live connection.

Disconnect and reconnect, built in Phase 5 but reachable only from connector detail, are now on every management row behind a
disclosure carrying the full consequence list and a required checkbox plus reason. Actions are derived from the persisted
lifecycle: **View access is never offered before discovery produced records**, and a superseded connector never offers reconnect.

`view.ts` (the demo-state marketplace resolver) is deleted — the marketplace consults no browser-local state at all.

18 action/card tests, 15 marketplace tests, 18 management tests. Five mutations caught: ignoring persisted Slack/Entra instances,
labelling configured as Connected, offering access before discovery, allowing reconnect of a superseded connector, and rendering a
read failure as "nothing configured". **Staging only.**

## PR #381 — Phase 5: enterprise connector management (2026-07-31)

Migration **0073**. The product assumed one directory; a workspace can have several, and they are separate organizations that are
never merged.

Disconnect joins supersession under one notion of "active" — `superseded_by` (replaced) or `disconnected_at` (retired, no
successor). Both are read-time exclusions: nothing is deleted, and reconnect restores by clearing a column. The active predicate
widened across all ten product read RPCs.

Connector scoping: the 0061 RPCs always accepted `p_connection_id` and nothing ever sent it. The DAL now does, and the scope lives
in the URL, so Home, Directory, Access and Findings cannot disagree. A global switcher renders only when there is more than one
active directory.

New surfaces: `/connectors/manage` (every directory, active and retired, with per-connector counts — never summed) and
`/connectors/manage/[id]` (status, verification, discovery history, health with a stated reason, and the three operator actions).

Eight groups against real Postgres, 18 UI tests, eight mutations caught — including disconnect deleting rows instead of excluding
them, the inventory hiding retired connectors, and counts being summed across organizations. **Staging only.**

## PR #380 — Phase 4: identity-graph cross-linking, Findings by subject (2026-07-31)

No migration. `subjectLink` gains GROUP subjects (they rendered as no link at all) and disambiguates ASSIGNMENT subjects by RULE —
`direct_assignment_with_stale_endpoint` carries an identity id, `group_assignment_with_stale_endpoint` a group id, and both are
uuids so the value cannot say which. `graph` findings stay unlinked: their subjectId is a sha256 scope token.

`GroupPathView` and `ApplicationAssignedGroupView` gain the canonical group id, so "Through &lt;group&gt;" on a person and the
assigned groups on an application become links. Routed on ids, never labels.

/access/findings is grouped into five subject buckets (People, Groups, Applications, Assignments, Connector & directory), ordered by
the worst severity each contains, severity still leading within each. Nothing is hidden — order and grouping only. A `subject` URL
filter composes with the existing severity/rule/subjectType filters.

Findings with no safe subject get a truthful explanation instead of a fabricated action.

Needs Attention integration DEFERRED with reason: it is six cheap parallel reads today, and consuming findings would add a counts
RPC plus up to six full table page-throughs.

Five mutations caught, including routing by display name and mapping an assignment finding to the wrong subject kind.

## PR #379 — Phase 3: first-class Group detail (2026-07-31)

Migration **0072** adds `product_group_access_subgraph` and `/directory/groups/[id]`. One RPC per page view; the existing Phase-13
access engine and Phase-14 governance engine do the interpretation, with a new `evaluateGroupGovernance` mirroring the identity
analogue rather than re-deriving the two group rules.

Connector-scoped edges (defence in depth over the composite FKs) and bounded inside the function, because a group is the fan-in
case. Supersession-gated anchor, so the P0 rule holds. The page answers the question the surface exists for: for each application
the group grants, how many members would keep it anyway through a direct assignment.

Eleven SQL groups plus 19 UI tests; eight mutations caught, including binding by name instead of id and truncating instead of
refusing a fan-in group. **Staging only.**

## PR #378 — P0: one Okta organization, one active connector (2026-07-31)

Migration **0071**. Two connectors in staging tenant `aaaa1111-…` read the same Okta organization, so Home, People, Groups,
Directory applications, Access and Findings all double-counted. Proven one organization by Okta external-id overlap — every legacy
id also present under the controlled connector, none unique to it.

`connectors` gains a declared `superseded_by` pointer (with timestamp and reason, all three enforced together); all nine 0061 read
RPCs exclude rows owned by a superseded connector, and both subgraph functions gate their anchor so a superseded record has no
detail page. No DISTINCT, no row-attribute dedup — the duplication is per connector, so the fix is per connector. Nothing deleted
or rewritten; the exclusion is read-time only and reversible. A second, genuinely different Okta organization stays fully visible.

`getOktaConnectorStatus` and `findOwnOktaConnector` also stop resolving superseded connectors, so the connector surface agrees with
the directory surfaces.

Eight groups against real Postgres, six mutations caught. `AA6` and `MM6` rescoped — both asserted global cross-suite counts.
**Staging only.**

## PR #377 — Phase 2.1: `current` → `stale_since IS NULL` invariant (2026-07-31)

Migration **0070**. `runner_promote_okta_directory_users` (0053) and `runner_promote_okta_directory_groups` (0054) restored a row to
`current` without clearing `stale_since`, unlike the other four promoters. A returning identity or group was `current` while carrying the
timestamp from when it went missing. Repairs all six tables (scoped to `current`, idempotent), replaces the two functions with a one-line
change each, and adds a validated CHECK to all six. A normalizing trigger was rejected in favour of the CHECK: it would silently mask
every future recurrence.

Eleven behavioural groups against real Postgres driving the real RPCs, plus a static guard for the repair's scope (unobservable in the
harness, which applies migrations to an empty database). Four mutations each caught, including widening the repair to still-stale rows —
which would erase last-seen evidence — and clearing `stale_since` tenant-wide on promote.

No threshold, breaker, gate, trigger or budget touched. **Staging only; not yet applied.** RISK-007 / Phase C posture unchanged.

### PR #180 — B2c-run operational runbook (first real-token event) · 2026-06-25

### PR #179 — Production-shaped synthetic OAuth callback route (B2c-route) · 2026-06-25
- **Category:** RISK-007 implementation — B2c-**route** (the production-shaped Slack OAuth callback route, fully synthetic + production-disabled). Deep-gated (public route + OAuth + session boundary). NO real Slack API call, NO real token, NO real client secret, NO request-path decrypt, NO route returning a decrypted token, NO live connector, NO production enablement. NO migration (tests 671 → **686**; RLS **620**; types **1955**); no doc 17 §5 box ticked.
- **`handleSyntheticSlackOAuthCallback(request, deps)`** (new server-only `oauth-callback-route-handler.ts`): the production request-path SHAPE — guard → explicit session resolution → parse → B2c-wire orchestrator → safe/static response — wired with FULLY SYNTHETIC/mocked deps.
- **Production-disabled guard (earliest refusal):** `isSyntheticCallbackEnabled` reads **trusted server env only** (false in production and without the explicit `CONNECTOR_OAUTH_SYNTHETIC_CALLBACK_ENABLED=1` staging opt-in; a request value cannot enable it). When disabled, the handler returns a **generic 404 BEFORE reading/parsing/logging any request material** — it does not reveal an OAuth callback, name the guard/env/config, or return a reason; the orchestrator is not called.
- **Explicit session resolution (no layout auth):** the handler resolves the completing subject via an injected `resolveSubject` (the route wires `getSessionUser`); no session / a resolution failure fails closed to a safe error. **Wrong session** (subject ≠ `state.sub`) is rejected by B2a (`subject_mismatch`) and never reaches the exchange.
- **Request-path discipline + safe responses:** only `state` + `code` are read from the query — never logged, echoed into an error, put in a response body/header, or returned. Responses are safe/static **303s to `/connectors?oauth=success|error`** (a coarse flag only — never raw code/state/reason). An orchestrator throw → safe error.
- **Upstream redirect discipline (proxy.ts — review fold):** the Proxy/middleware `updateSession` redirected an unauthenticated request for a protected route to `/login` while **preserving the original query** — so `/connectors/oauth/callback?code=X&state=Y` became `/login?code=X&state=Y`, leaking the raw `code`/`state` into the `/login` URL (access logs, history, referer, analytics) **before** the handler guard runs. Fixed: the redirect now **drops the query** (`redirectUrl.search = ""`) — realizing the design intent that the in-flight `code`/`state` are dropped (`/login` reads only `?error=`). 3 `proxy.test.ts` tests prove the callback (and any protected route's query) redirects to a clean `/login`. No other upstream layer logs the callback URL/query (no `console`/logger touches `request.url`/`searchParams`; `@vercel/analytics`'s `<Analytics/>` is a page-layout client component, not run for the API route).
- **No egress / no client-secret decrypt:** the route wires `makeSyntheticOrchestratorRunner` (a synthetic http client returning a token-shaped `xoxb-…MUSTNOTLEAK…` sentinel, a synthetic client-secret sentinel, a synthetic store) — it has **no global `fetch`**, can never reach `slack.com`, and **does NOT import or call `withSlackClientSecret`/the B2c-secret decrypt boundary** (the synthetic exchange needs no real client-secret decrypt). The no-client-import guard now allows only the callback route to import the route handler.
- **Tests (synthetic — 12):** marked code/state/session/token/client-secret sentinels. Cover: production-disabled → generic 404, no disclosure, orchestrator not called, no request material logged; the trusted-env guard (production + missing opt-in disabled); no-session / session-failure → safe error, orchestrator not called; success/failure → safe 303 with no raw detail; an orchestrator throw → safe error; **end-to-end with the REAL orchestrator + synthetic deps** — a valid request reaches the (synthetic) exchange and **global fetch is never called**, while tampered state / wrong session / missing code do **NOT** reach the exchange (B2a/B2c-wire gate); an import-boundary scan (no `withSlackClientSecret`/`slack-client-secret-store`/decrypt/`fetch(`/`@slack/`).
- **Posture:** **B2c-route only — production-shaped but SYNTHETIC + production-disabled. No real Slack API call happened, no real token entered the system, no real client secret entered the system.** The **first real-token event remains FUTURE + explicitly authorized (B2c-run)**; **B2c-run remains future**. Live connector use remains **BLOCKED**. **Connector credentials are not production-ready. RISK-001 remains OPEN. RISK-007 remains OPEN. Cutover remains BLOCKED.** Updated docs 42 (§90), 44 (§7 — B2c-route done), 41 (§104), 09, 00.
- **Validation (local):** `npm test` **686/686** (54 files); lint clean; `tsc --noEmit` clean; `next build` clean; `check-auth-safety`/`check-migration-safety`/`pr-review-summary` pass; `test-rls.sh` **620** (no migration); `gen-types-local.sh` 0-diff (1955); `check-no-real-tokens.sh selftest` + `--all` pass; ref staging `ycdpzduxugdsffjqyoai` (local read); no new dependency.

### PR #178 — Vault-grade Slack client-secret store (B2c-secret) · 2026-06-25
- **Category:** RISK-007 implementation — B2c-**secret** (the OAuth master credential's vault-grade store, synthetic only). Standalone, deep-gated. NO real Slack client secret, NO real token, NO Slack API call, NO production callback route, NO live connector, NO request-path decrypt, NO production enablement. Tests 657 → **671**; RLS **614 → 620** (T56); migration `0035` (new table → types **1901 → 1955**); no doc 17 §5 box ticked.
- **App-scoped, not tenant-scoped:** the Slack client secret is the OAuth **master credential** (converts auth codes → tokens; app-wide blast radius). New `connector_app_secrets` table (migration `0035`) keyed by `{app_env, provider, secret_kind, version}` — **NO tenant_id**, append-only/versioned. **RLS deny-all to the request path** (RLS-enabled, ZERO policies, `revoke all` from authenticated/anon); **runner column-scoped grant only** (the same `connector_runner` BYPASSRLS + KMS boundary as the bot-token vault). RLS **T56** proves authenticated/anon cannot read/insert and the runner has no update/delete grant. `test-rls.sh` re-revokes it after the harness's hosted-sim blanket grant (lockstep, like `connector_secrets`).
- **App-scope AAD (`crypto.ts`):** refactored to a shared envelope core (`sealEnvelope`/`openEnvelope` over an AAD buffer — the existing tenant path delegates to it, 16 crypto tests unchanged) + a new **app-scope AAD** (`canonicalAppAad`, a **distinct domain prefix** binding `{app_env, provider, kind, version}`). A staging ciphertext **cannot** decrypt as production, and a wrong provider/kind/version fails closed.
- **Decrypt boundary (load-bearing) — `withSlackClientSecret(identity, deps, exchange)`:** there is deliberately **NO `loadClientSecret(): string` API**. The closure decrypts the secret, hands the plaintext **only** to the `exchange` callback (the runner-side Slack exchange), wipes the buffer immediately after, and returns **only** the callback's redacted result. The plaintext is never returned, logged, audited, thrown, or persisted. Decrypt runs through the injected runner-backed store + KMS provider — not the web/request identity, arbitrary app code, or any route. `saveSlackClientSecret` stores the envelope only (no plaintext at rest, redacted ref).
- **Tests (synthetic — 14):** a **marked** client-secret sentinel (`MUSTNOTLEAK-…`). Cover: app-scope AAD round-trip + staging≠production + wrong provider/kind/version fail closed; envelope-only at rest (no plaintext in row/return); the secret reaches **only** the exchange callback and appears in no result/log/error; **a forced post-decrypt exchange failure (the callback throws with the secret embedded) does not leak it** (fail-closed `use_failed`); an identity without `kms:Decrypt` cannot decrypt; a staging-sealed envelope cannot be used under a production identity; `not_found`; the runner-backed store INSERT goes under `SET ROLE connector_runner`, targets the granted columns, and carries ciphertext (not the plaintext); a runner round-trip; a source scan proving no `load/get/read/fetchClientSecret` API + no `process.env`.
- **Posture:** **B2c-secret only — no real Slack client secret entered the system, no real token entered the system, no real Slack API call happened.** The **app-secret USE audit (§90.7) remains FUTURE** (not implemented; docs say so). The **production callback route remains FUTURE (B2c-route)**; the **first real-token event remains FUTURE + explicitly authorized (B2c-run)**. Live connector use remains **BLOCKED**. **Connector credentials are not production-ready. RISK-001 remains OPEN. RISK-007 remains OPEN. Cutover remains BLOCKED.** Updated docs 42 (§90.3), 44 (§7 — B2c-secret done), 41 (§103), 09, 00.
- **Validation (local):** `npm test` **671/671** (52 files); lint clean; `tsc --noEmit` clean; `next build` clean; `check-auth-safety`/`check-migration-safety`/`pr-review-summary` pass; `test-rls.sh` **620** (`ALL ORG-RLS ASSERTIONS PASSED`); `gen-types-local.sh` stable (1955 — the new table); `check-no-real-tokens.sh selftest` + `--all` pass; ref staging `ycdpzduxugdsffjqyoai` (local read); no new dependency.

### PR #176 — Synthetic OAuth callback wiring (B2c-wire) · 2026-06-25
- **Category:** RISK-007 implementation — B2c-**wire** (synthetic end-to-end callback composition only). New server-only `oauth-callback-orchestrator.ts`. **B2c is split into FOUR never-combined steps: B2c-wire (this) / B2c-secret (real KMS client-secret store — future) / B2c-route (synthetic production callback route, request-path discipline proven — future) / B2c-run (first real-token event — future, explicitly authorized).** NO real Slack API call, NO real token, NO real client secret, NO route, NO request-path decrypt, NO live connector, NO production enablement. NO migration (tests 630 → **657**; RLS **614**; types **1901**); no doc 17 §5 box ticked.
- **`orchestrateSlackOAuthCallback(query, deps)`** composes the three already-proven pieces into ONE pure-function path: **B2a `validateOAuthState` (the authoritative GATE) → B2b `exchangeSlackOAuthCode` (MOCKED) → B1 store/encrypt**. It is a pure function — **no production OAuth callback route was added.**
- **Single source of truth:** after validation succeeds, every security-bound field threaded downstream (tenant, connector, exact redirect, correlation) comes ONLY from the **validated state payload** (`v.payload`). The untrusted callback query is read ONLY for `state` + `code` (asserted by a source scan); a decoy tenant/connector/provider in the query is ignored — proven by a test that puts decoys in the query and asserts the store binds the validated tenant/connector.
- **Causal gating:** `validateOAuthState`'s `ok` is the ONLY thing that unlocks the exchange (validation is authoritative, not advisory — its `payload` drives the flow). The exchange stage is unreachable for any state that did not pass validation (asserted: a tampered/invalid state → the injected http client has 0 calls). Stage ordering validate→exchange→store, each stage skipped if the prior fails.
- **Exchange-success/store-failure seam (orchestration layer):** when the mocked exchange succeeds (returns the token-shaped sentinel) but the store fails, the orchestrator returns a safe `store_failed` and the token appears nowhere (result/error/console) — the orchestrator never sees the token (B2b never returns it). Distinct from B1's internal DB/audit atomicity.
- **Real B1 path:** `b1StoreHandoff(deps)` wires the **real** `ingestStagingConnectorSecret` as the B2b store handoff; a test drives the token-shaped sentinel through the real envelope path and proves only **ciphertext** is stored (the sentinel never appears in the committed INSERT statements) and a redacted ref returns; a production-blocked B1 fails closed without leaking the token.
- **Tests (synthetic — 27):** use three detectable sentinels — token `xoxb-…MUSTNOTLEAK…`, a marked client-secret, a marked code — and the **REAL** B2a validator (states minted by `createOAuthState`). Cover: happy path → redacted ref; SSOT decoy-ignored + source scan; stage-skip on each failure; the full B2a state-integration matrix (tampered/malformed/missing/wrong-session/no-session/wrong-tenant/connector/redirect/expired/**replayed** → no exchange); every failure seam (missing/denied client secret, malformed/missing-token/network, store failure) fails closed with no leak; the exchange-success/store-failure token drop; global fetch stubbed to fail loud + asserted never called; the real-B1 envelope path.
- **Posture:** **B2c-wire only — no real Slack call happened, no real token entered the system, no real client secret entered the system.** The **KMS-backed client-secret store remains FUTURE (B2c-secret)**; the **production OAuth callback route remains FUTURE (B2c-route)**; the **first real-token event remains FUTURE + explicitly authorized (B2c-run)**. Real OAuth exchange / live connector remain **BLOCKED**. **Connector credentials are not production-ready. RISK-001 remains OPEN. RISK-007 remains OPEN. Cutover remains BLOCKED.** Updated docs 42 (§90), 44 (§7 — B2c split), 41 (§102), 09, 00.
- **Validation (local):** `npm test` **657/657** (51 files); lint clean; `tsc --noEmit` clean; `next build` clean; `check-auth-safety`/`check-migration-safety`/`pr-review-summary` pass; `test-rls.sh` **614** (no migration); `gen-types-local.sh` 0-diff (1901); `check-no-real-tokens.sh selftest` + `--all` pass; ref staging `ycdpzduxugdsffjqyoai` (local read); no new dependency.

### PR #175 — Mocked Slack OAuth exchange wrapper (B2b) · 2026-06-25
- **Category:** RISK-007 implementation — B2b (mocked exchange wrapper only). New server-only `slack-oauth-exchange.ts`. NO real Slack API call, NO real token, NO callback route that performs a real exchange, NO live connector, NO request-path decrypt, NO production enablement. NO migration (tests 612 → **630**; RLS **614**; types **1901**); no doc 17 §5 box ticked.
- **`exchangeSlackOAuthCode(input, deps)`** (docs/42 §90.4): takes a **B2a-validated** authorization `code` + the EXACT redirect URI (caller precondition — never re-derived from request data), reads the client secret from an **INJECTED `ClientSecretProvider`** (NEVER `process.env`), POSTs `client_id/client_secret/code/redirect_uri` to the Slack token endpoint (`SLACK_TOKEN_URL` = `https://slack.com/api/oauth.v2.access`) via an **INJECTED `SlackHttpClient`**, parses the bot-token response in memory, and hands the token STRAIGHT to the B1 store/encrypt handoff (`ExchangeStoreHandoff`). Returns a **REDACTED ref** on success.
- **No leak:** the `code`, client secret, raw Slack response, and bot token are NEVER logged, echoed into an error, returned, or audited. Slack `error` codes are sanitized to safe static reason classes (never echoed). Fail-closed reasons: `missing_client_secret` / `client_secret_denied` / `exchange_http_error` / `slack_error` / `malformed_response` / `missing_bot_token` / `unexpected_token_type` / `store_failed`. The live token reference is dropped after the handoff (V8-heap residual remains — not a hard wipe, docs/44 §2 step 7).
- **No network:** the module has NO global `fetch` and NO fallback — the injected client is the only Slack-calling path. Tests stub global `fetch` to **fail loud** and assert it is never called, so an automated test cannot accidentally hit `slack.com`.
- **Tests (synthetic):** 18 tests use a **TOKEN-SHAPED** `xoxb-…MUSTNOTLEAK…` sentinel (a real Slack-`xoxb-`-shaped value carrying the approved synthetic marker — NOT a generic placeholder) so the no-leak assertions prove a real-shaped token would not survive into result/error/logs. Cover: valid response → store handoff + redacted ref (no token); `ok:false` sanitized; missing/non-bot token; malformed/non-object body; network/timeout; non-2xx; store failure; **missing/denied client secret** (provider throws — secret not surfaced even in the thrown error); forced post-secret-read failure doesn't surface the secret; **global fetch never called**; missing injected deps fail loud; the `code` is never logged/returned; extra Slack fields ignored; server-only source scan (no `fetch(`/`process.env`/decrypt/route/real-token).
- **Posture:** **B2b is a MOCKED exchange wrapper. No real Slack API call happened; no real token entered the system.** The **vault-grade/KMS-backed client-secret store remains FUTURE** (B2c — the provider is injected/mocked here); **exchange-specific audit (`connector_oauth.exchange.*`) remains FUTURE** (not implemented in B2b). **Real OAuth exchange remains BLOCKED; live connector use remains BLOCKED.** **Connector credentials are not production-ready. RISK-001 remains OPEN. RISK-007 remains OPEN. Cutover remains BLOCKED.** Updated docs 42 (§90.4), 44 (§7), 41 (§101), 09, 00.
- **Validation (local):** `npm test` **630/630** (50 files); lint clean; `tsc --noEmit` clean; `next build` clean; `check-auth-safety`/`check-migration-safety`/`pr-review-summary` pass; `test-rls.sh` **614** (no migration); `gen-types-local.sh` 0-diff (1901); `check-no-real-tokens.sh selftest` + `--all` pass (the `xoxb` sentinel is excused by its `MUSTNOTLEAK` marker); ref staging `ycdpzduxugdsffjqyoai` (local read); no new dependency.

### PR #174 — OAuth state binding + validation (B2a) · 2026-06-25
- **Category:** RISK-007 implementation — B2a (state generation/validation only). Closes the docs/42 §90.2 `validateOAuthState` binding gap. NO Slack OAuth exchange, NO Slack API call, NO callback route that exchanges a code, NO real token, NO live connector, NO request-path decrypt, NO production enablement. Tests 585 → **612**; RLS **611 → 614** (T55); migration `0034` (0-diff types — `1901`); no doc 17 §5 box ticked.
- **Eight-field binding (`oauth-state.ts`):** the signed payload now carries `redir` (EXACT redirect URI) + `corr` (correlation/operation id) in addition to `sub`/`tid`/`prov`/`cid`/`exp`/`nonce`. `createOAuthState` requires the actor subject + an absolute-HTTPS `redirectUri` + a grammar-safe `correlationId` (else throws, no state).
- **Per-field validation (`validateOAuthState`):** when a completing context is supplied it compares EACH bound field against the completing request/session and FAILS CLOSED on ANY mismatch — new safe reason codes `session_required` (no completing actor/session), `subject_mismatch`, `redirect_uri_mismatch`, `correlation_mismatch` (plus the existing tenant/provider/connector/expired/replayed/malformed). A state missing `sub`/`redir`/`corr` is `malformed_state`.
- **Generation-time authorization (`generateBoundOAuthState`):** an injected `authorizeActor` gate verifies the initiating actor is authorized for the bound tenant + connector — an actor CANNOT mint a state for a tenant/connector they cannot access, and the signer is **never invoked** on failure (no usable state). This prevents a self-consistent malicious state; the callback equality check prevents cross-state confusion; both are required.
- **Exact redirect, server-trusted (`serverTrustedRedirectUri`):** the redirect compared at callback is resolved ONLY from server config (absolute HTTPS), **NEVER** reconstructed from request-controlled inputs (Host, X-Forwarded-Host, Forwarded, request URL, origin, query). Tests prove a spoofed Host cannot make a mismatched redirect validate (it fails closed).
- **Atomic single-use reused:** the existing `oauth-pending-consume.ts` atomic consume (one `UPDATE … where consumed_at is null … returning`; concurrent second consume fails closed) is the single-use marker — no new race-prone code. Migration `0034` widens the `oauth_pending.last_rejected_code` CHECK for the four new codes (RLS **T55**: runner records them; an out-of-allowlist value is rejected).
- **Provider wiring:** `buildSlackAuthorizeUrl` / `persistSlackAuthorizePending` now require + bind the actor `subject`, the exact `redirectUri`, and a `correlationId` (the authorize redirect IS the bound redirect).
- **Tests (synthetic):** 26 new binding tests — generation binds all eight fields; generation-auth rejects an unauthorized tenant/connector; **one isolated per-field mismatch test ×8** (the other seven match, so removing any single comparison fails a specific test); confused-deputy (attacker-state/victim-session, tenant/connector/redirect/provider swap); expired; replayed; no-session/session_required; Host/X-Forwarded-Host spoof cannot satisfy redirect; safe/static reasons with no raw state/session in the result.
- **Posture:** **B2a is state generation/validation only. Slack exchange remains BLOCKED; a callback route that calls Slack remains BLOCKED; real token remains BLOCKED; live connector use remains BLOCKED; the Slack client-secret vault-grade store remains future/not implemented.** **Connector credentials are not production-ready. RISK-001 remains OPEN. RISK-007 remains OPEN. Cutover remains BLOCKED.** Updated docs 42 (§90.2), 44 (§7), 41 (§100), 09, 00.
- **Validation (local):** `npm test` **612/612** (49 files); lint clean; `tsc --noEmit` clean; `next build` clean; `check-auth-safety`/`check-migration-safety`/`pr-review-summary` pass; `test-rls.sh` **614** (`ALL ORG-RLS ASSERTIONS PASSED`); `gen-types-local.sh` 0-diff (1901); `check-no-real-tokens.sh selftest` + `--all` pass; ref staging `ycdpzduxugdsffjqyoai` (local read); no new dependency.

### PR #173 — Slack OAuth authorize/callback/exchange design gate (B2, docs only) · 2026-06-25
- **Category:** RISK-007 design gate (docs only — NO OAuth implementation, callback route, Slack API call, real token, live connector, request-path decrypt, or production enablement). Designs the Slack OAuth authorize→callback→code-exchange path in **doc 42 §90** + folds the threat/evidence/sequence into **doc 44** (§2/§5/§7). Tests/RLS/types unchanged (585 / 611 / 1901); no doc 17 §5 box ticked.
- **doc 42 §90 (the design):** (1) **code-vs-token boundary** — the callback receives a one-time authorization `code` on the request path (NOT the token); it is validated + state-bound then handed to a SERVER-SIDE exchange; the `code`→token `oauth.v2.access` exchange is runner-only; the bot token is born server-side + encrypted IMMEDIATELY via the existing vault and NEVER returns toward the browser; no path exposes the token after exchange. (2) **state/CSRF/binding** — binds tenant/provider/connector + (the **B2a gap to close**) **actor/session (`sub`) + EXACT redirect URI** (no prefix/substring/origin/loose), single-use `nonce_hash`, short TTL — closes replay/CSRF/tenant-swap/connector-swap/wrong-user/victim-session/redirect-confusion/stale-code. The current `validateOAuthState` binds tenant/provider/connector but NOT `sub`/intent — flagged for B2a. (3) **Slack client secret = VAULT-GRADE** — KMS-backed, runner-only, ≥ bot-token strength, NEVER a plaintext env var (it mints tokens → its leak is app-wide). (4) **exchange path** (`oauth.v2.access`, runner identity, no token/response logging, sanitized errors, fail-closed, bot-token-only, refresh out of scope). (5) **receipt-to-encryption zero-observer plan** (access-log/Referer surfaces for the `code`; no server tracer/error-vendor exists). (6) **audit events** (authorize/callback/exchange + store) — never raw code/response/token/secret. (7) **fail-closed failure modes**. (8) **sequence** B2a→B2b→B2c→B2d.
- **doc 44 updates:** §2 — the **three secrets traced separately** (auth `code`, Slack token, client secret); §7 — the PR sequence **split B2 into B2 design → B2a (state validation + actor/exact-redirect binding) → B2b (mock exchange) → B2c (first real-token event, explicitly authorized) → B2d (live connector)**; §5 (OAuth sub-block) — the pre-first-OAuth evidence gate (staging flag, dev workspace/app, source-revocable token, named revocation operator, no-production/exact redirect URI, log surfaces, scanner, **client secret vault-grade + not in plaintext env**, runbooks, explicit Sam authorization).
- **Posture:** **B2 design only; OAuth implementation remains blocked; real token remains blocked; the first real-token event (B2c) remains future + explicitly authorized; live connector use remains blocked; the Slack client secret is vault-grade and must be protected before real exchange.** **Connector credentials are not production-ready. RISK-001 remains OPEN. RISK-007 remains OPEN. Cutover remains BLOCKED.** Updated docs 42 (§90), 44 (§2/§5/§7), 09, 00, 41 (§99).

### PR #172 — Staging-only store/encrypt ingestion path (B1, synthetic only) · 2026-06-25
- **Category:** RISK-007 implementation — B1 (the **store/encrypt half** of doc 44 §2, **synthetic only**). New server-only `connector-secret-ingest.ts` (`ingestStagingConnectorSecret`) + threading a grammar-safe `correlation_id` into the existing store audit + `scripts/check-no-real-tokens.sh`. **No migration, no schema/types/RLS change** (tests 571 → **585**; RLS still **611**; types **1901**); no doc 17 §5 box ticked.
- **Build split (doc 44 §7, B1/B2):** B1 builds the store/encrypt half with **synthetic sentinel values only**; the OAuth `oauth.v2.access` exchange + the first real-token event are **deferred to B2** (token born server-side, no human handling — doc 44's no-human-handling property preserved). **Merging B1 does NOT authorize a real-token run.**
- **Ingestion wrapper (`connector-secret-ingest.ts`):** the smallest guarded entry that encrypts + stores through the existing `saveConnectorSecret` (encrypt-only provider → atomic store + audit). Fail-closed guards IN ORDER: (1) **production hard-block** `isStagingIngestEnvironment()` — requires the explicit `CONNECTOR_VAULT_STAGING_INGEST_ENABLED=1` opt-in AND a non-production env (`VERCEL_ENV`/`NODE_ENV` ≠ production); fail-closed default-off; read from the trusted server env, never request data; (2) **provider/kind allowlist** — Slack dev-workspace bot OAuth access token ONLY; (3) required `tenant_id`/`connector_id` (uuid) + positive-int `version`; (4) **grammar-safe `correlation_id`** (reuses the #166 grammar via the new `isSafeCorrelationId`). On success → encrypt-immediately + atomic store + `store.attempted/succeeded` audit (carrying the correlation_id) + a redacted `SavedSecretRef`. **Plaintext is never logged/echoed/returned**; on any guard/encrypt/audit failure NOTHING commits (no secret row, no succeeded audit) and a static error is thrown.
- **Plaintext handling (doc 44 §2):** B1 has NO HTTP route, so there is no request-body observer surface (middleware/logger/tracer/body-parser) — the receipt-to-encryption observer analysis lands with B2's callback route. The wrapper receives the plaintext as a function arg and hands it straight to the encrypt call; the live reference is dropped after encrypt, V8 heap residual remains (NOT a hard wipe), exactly as doc 44 §2 step 7 documents.
- **Secret scanner (`scripts/check-no-real-tokens.sh`):** scans **changed files** (incl. untracked; `--all` for the whole tree) for HIGH-CONFIDENCE full-token shapes — Slack `xox[baprs]-`/`xapp-`, JWTs, `AKIA`/`ASIA`, PEM private keys, `gho_`/`github_pat_`, `sk-`/`sk-proj-`, and DB URLs with an embedded password to a non-local, non-`example.` host — redacts matches, exits non-zero on a hit, and **fails loud** if perl is absent. Decisions are **token-scoped** (a match is excused only if the matched TOKEN itself carries a sentinel marker — `SENTINEL`/`EXAMPLE`/`LEAK…`), NOT line-scoped, so a real token is still caught on a JSX/generic/`// placeholder` line; **no whole-file allowlist** (only the scanner skips itself), so a real token in any file is caught. A `selftest` subcommand proves 7 positives are caught (incl. real tokens with a sentinel marker sitting **adjacent** — proving the excuse is token-intrinsic, NOT proximity-based) + 6 sentinels excused only by their own intrinsic marker. **Wired into CI** (`review-discipline.yml`: `selftest` + `--all`).
- **Tests (synthetic sentinels only):** allowed ingestion stores envelope-only + no token anywhere (row/ref/audits/console); forbidden provider/kind rejected; missing/invalid ids + version rejected; invalid correlation_id rejected; **production guard refuses** (no row, no audit, no DB touch, returns nothing); audit-failure fails closed (atomic rollback, no secret row); encryption-failure fails closed; the module imports only sibling vault modules and contains no `fetch`/decrypt/`oauth.v2.access`/Slack-API/route/`xoxb-`.
- **Posture:** **B1 is synthetic-only and authorizes no real-token run.** NO real provider token. NO operator/admin-console paste. NO OAuth exchange. NO Slack API call. NO callback route. NO live connector. NO request-path decrypt. NO route returning a decrypted token. NO production enablement. NO rotation helper. Real credential save/load/use is still missing; the first real-token event is deferred to B2. **Connector credentials are not production-ready. RISK-001 remains OPEN. RISK-007 remains OPEN. Cutover remains BLOCKED.** Updated docs 44 (§2/§7), 42 (§89), 41 (§98), 09, 00.

### PR #170 — Implement revoke/tombstone lifecycle write helpers + atomic lifecycle audit · 2026-06-24
- **Category:** RISK-007 implementation (the lifecycle WRITE side). Tests 559 → **571**; RLS suite **571 → 611** (T53 updated + T54); `database.types.ts` **1901** (grant-only 0-diff); migration `0033`; no doc 17 §5 box ticked.
- **Grant (`0033`, INSERT-only):** column-scoped `connector_runner` INSERT on `connector_secret_lifecycle_events` of EXACTLY the eight safe-metadata columns `(tenant_id, connector_id, secret_kind, version, lifecycle_event_type, reason_class, actor_type, correlation_id)`. Keeps the #169 SELECT; NO UPDATE/DELETE; NOT `id`/`created_at` (server defaults) or `audit_log_id`. `connector_secrets` is UNTOUCHED (no new grant, no constraint) — T50 holds.
- **Helpers (the load-bearing claim) — ONE atomic CTE per call:** `ins_lifecycle` (`INSERT … WHERE EXISTS (the connector_secrets row) RETURNING version` = the SINGLE existence determination / source of truth) + an UNCONDITIONAL `attempted` audit + `succeeded` gated `WHERE EXISTS (select 1 from ins_lifecycle)` + `failed`(`target_not_found`) gated `WHERE NOT EXISTS (select 1 from ins_lifecycle)`. succeeded/failed are mutually exclusive BY CONSTRUCTION (both derive from `ins_lifecycle`, not a second predicate) → **exactly one terminal per op**. A **nonexistent target → the helper THROWS** (caller NEVER receives `{ ok }`); NO lifecycle row, NO succeeded; `attempted` + `failed` ARE audited (the failed attempt is auditable). The **orphan invariant binds lifecycle ROWS** (never reference a nonexistent version), NOT the attempted/failed AUDIT rows. One statement → any insert failure rolls back the WHOLE statement (no lifecycle row without its audit, no compensating DELETE) → fail closed. Helpers return `{ ok }` / throw — never a secret/envelope/key material.
- **T54 — real DB, under `set role connector_runner`:** (1) GRANT SHAPE (exactly the 8 INSERT cols; no table-level INSERT; no UPDATE/DELETE; can't INSERT id/created_at/audit_log_id; `connector_secrets` still no runner UPDATE/DELETE); (2) APPEND-ONLY STILL HOLDS now that the runner has INSERT (runner UPDATE/DELETE fail; the trigger STILL rejects a privileged role's UPDATE/DELETE; row unchanged/still exists after); (3) the ACTUAL CTE — EXISTING v55 → lifecycle + attempted + succeeded, no failed, one terminal; NONEXISTENT v777 → attempted + failed(`target_not_found`), NO lifecycle/succeeded, one terminal; (4) ATOMICITY — a forced in-CTE succeeded-audit failure rolls back the lifecycle insert.
- **Posture:** **revoke/tombstone write helpers implemented (atomic with audit, exactly one terminal, nonexistent fails explicitly, no orphan lifecycle rows). NO rotation helper. No real provider token. No OAuth/token exchange. No live connector. No request-path decrypt. No service-role secret path. No public route. No UPDATE/DELETE on `connector_secrets` or the lifecycle table. No hard delete.** The #169 load semantics are unchanged. Real credentials remain blocked; real credential save/load/use is still missing; rotation + the rest of the real credential lifecycle remain missing. **Connector credentials are not production-ready. RISK-001 remains OPEN. RISK-007 remains OPEN. Cutover remains BLOCKED.** Updated docs 42 (§87), 41 (§96), 03, 09, 00 + `rls_test_plan.md`.
