Filed alongside the O2E Phase 0 inventory, which found scheduled sync blocked: the slot machinery is Entra-only.


## O2D.2 — stale-transition audit trail, migration 0068 (v3 #368)

**2026-07-31.** Closes the gap O2D.1 exposed: a controlled group was staled correctly and left no `audit_logs` row, because the
canonical directory tables carried no audit trigger.

One `SECURITY DEFINER` trigger function plus six triggers, gated on `WHEN (old.sync_status = 'current' and new.sync_status =
'stale')`. The six stale RPCs are untouched. Bounded payload with an exact asserted key set; `last_seen_run_id` named precisely
for what it is rather than presented as the staling run.

Mutation-verified: disabling the insert, dropping the WHEN clause, duplicating the event, adding provider data, and removing
connector scoping each break the suite.

Also extends `scripts/test-rls.sh` to revoke browser-role EXECUTE on TRIGGER-returning functions. Its blanket
`grant execute on all functions` masked the migration's revoke — the same masking class already fixed for `runner_*`.

## O2D.1 COMPLETE — production budget configured and controlled stale marking verified (v3 #367)

**2026-07-31.** One complete five-sweep discovery on the production budget staled exactly one controlled group.

| | |
|---|---|
| groups sweep | `dbaa4843…` — seen 6, created 4, updated 2, **staleCandidates 1, groupsMarkedStale 1**, breaker **not** triggered |
| staled row | `zz-idcaddie-stale-test` — `sync_status: stale`, `stale_since: 2026-07-31 17:16:31`, row PRESENT |
| unchanged | all six live groups `current`; identities/apps/memberships/assignments **0 stale** |
| all five runs | `succeeded` / `complete=true` / `last_page` / `rejected=0` / `review_required=false` |

**The circuit breaker's denominator is post-promotion, not pre-run.** An earlier attempt (3 prior current, 1 absent = 33% > 30%)
tripped the breaker and marked zero — correctly. Adding four disposable groups moved the denominator to 6, so 1 absent = 16.7% and
the real stale path executed. The 30% threshold was NEVER changed; the org was made large enough for the guard to permit the write.

**Budget:** first live stale run used peak 6 pages of 400 (1.5%) and 6 records of 80,000. Selection and boundedness are proven;
scale is not.

**GAP — stale transitions are NOT audited.** `directory_groups` (and the other canonical directory tables) carry no audit
trigger; only `okta_connector_capability_evidence` does. Forensic evidence exists — `stale_since`, `updated_at`,
`last_discovery_run_id` still pointing at the run that last SAW the group, `connector_run_discovery` metrics, and the task's
`groupsMarkedStale` — but there is no `audit_logs` row for a row leaving `current`. For a signal that feeds access decisions that
is a real hole, and it should be closed before scheduled sync makes staling routine and unattended.

## O2D COMPLETE — one controlled initial discovery on staging (v3 #365)

**2026-07-31.** Five resource sweeps for controlled connector `cdf19b61…`, image `sha256:4c08b409…`, all complete and clean.

| sweep | run | pages | seen | rejected | created | stale |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| users | `e86af420…` | 1 | 1 | 0 | 1 identity | 0 |
| groups | `ab906d2e…` | 1 | 2 | 0 | 2 groups | 0 |
| memberships | `439a691e…` | 2 | 1 | 0 | 1 edge | 0 |
| applications | `ba337d74…` | 1 | 2 | 0 | 2 apps | 0 |
| assignments | `ae7f2867…` | 4 | 1 | 0 | 1 user edge | 0 |

All five `succeeded` / `completeness=true` / `termination_reason=last_page` / `review_required=false`. Connector advanced
`verified → discovery_pending → discovering → discovered`; `status` stays `pending`, certification_only true, production disabled.

**Five separate connector_runs, deliberately.** `connector_run_discovery` is `unique (run_id)` with no resource discriminator, so
one shared run across five sweeps would have had the last writer own the run's completeness — letting a complete sweep authorise
staling for an incomplete one. One run per sweep IS the gate's unit of accounting.

**Legacy isolation held byte-for-byte.** All 7 legacy rows compared on id, connection_id, sync_status, updated_at and stale_since
after every sweep: unchanged throughout. Zero cross-connector reassignment, zero stale marks anywhere.

**App-group assignments: complete-empty.** Both apps enumerated (`applicationsProcessed: 2`, 2 pages), zero group assignments
org-wide. Proven by full enumeration, not inferred from the earlier smoke.

Zero orphan edges, zero duplicate canonical rows, zero raw payloads in facts. Five KMS Signs matching the five task ids. Legacy
secret `LastAccessed` unchanged at 2026-07-23 with zero reads; the 5 GetSecretValue events are DB-URL injections, one per task.

**Cleanup debt:** seven `connector_runs` left `running` by the earlier bounded smokes (they open a run but never finish one). They
carry no `connector_run_discovery` row so they cannot satisfy the stale gate. Untouched by this phase, per instruction.

## O2C.4 — capability vocabulary for memberships and assignments (v3 #363, runner #111)

**2026-07-30.** Migration 0066 widens the bounded capability vocabulary by three: `group_memberships_read`,
`app_user_assignments_read`, `app_group_assignments_read`. Nothing else about the evidence model changes.

The runner gains a linked bounded smoke: two calls per capability (a fixed prerequisite `?limit=1` to obtain a parent id from the
authorized org, then the fixed target read), one token, one KMS Sign. The prerequisite exists because the controlled connector has
no persisted resources and the tenant's persisted groups/apps belong to a different connector whose org binding cannot be
verified.

Scopes are taken from the production readers: memberships is `okta.groups.read` despite returning users; both app surfaces are
`okta.apps.read`. All inside the approved three-scope contract — no scope gap.

## O2C.3 — per-capability evidence, contract 1.2.0 (v3 #361, runner #109)

**2026-07-30.** Contract moves to **1.2.0** recording `live_kid_verification: verified`, and migration 0065 adds
`okta_connector_capability_evidence` so each read scope carries its own proof rather than sharing one status field.

The version bump and the database pin move together by necessity: 0064 pins the accepted contract version, so bumping the
artifact without moving the pin would silently reject every future submission.

Existing users_read evidence is backfilled from the O2C.2 run — same run id, KID and timestamp, contract version left at 1.1.0
because that is what it was proven under.

## O2C.2 — Okta validation-result write path, migration 0064 (v3 #359)

**2026-07-30.** Closes the first of two gaps left by the O2C.2 live verification: the authentication succeeded against Okta but
the connector still read `never_validated`, because 0063 deliberately had no path capable of recording verification.

`runner_record_okta_connector_validation(...)` is granted to `connector_runner` only. Validation success is observed solely by the
runner, so no browser role can assert it.

**Security finding.** `revoke ... from public` does not achieve runner-only on Supabase — ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES grants EXECUTE
on new public functions to `anon`/`authenticated`/`service_role` as explicit grantees. The first run of the new suite showed an
OWNER recording a validation result. Each role is now revoked by name. **The pre-existing `runner_*` functions share the pattern**
and warrant their own pass; they are SECURITY DEFINER with internal tenant checks, so exposure is bounded.

Additive only; no new lifecycle vocabulary. `signing_key_id` reused as the verified KID. Expected KID pinned as a table CHECK so
not even a superuser can record another. Success evidence is all-or-nothing. Six mutations verified to break the suite.

Additive; activates nothing. Per this GO: RISK-007 OPEN, Phase C BLOCKED, staging only, production untouched.

## P5E14 — connector customer-pilot control plane (Gate S3 readiness) · 2026-07-14

Migration `0047` adds the provider-neutral **customer-pilot** control plane: 5 Tier-2 deny-all tables (`connector_pilot_enrollments`/
`_consents`/`_incidents`/`_exit_reviews`/`_deletion_jobs`) + 16 `SECURITY DEFINER` functions (11 `admin_*`→service_role, 4
`runner_*`→connector_runner, 1 internal opaque-reference guard), and a `'pilot'` scope on `0044`'s `connector_kill_switches` CHECK.
Deny-all + fail-closed throughout: RLS + zero policies + revoke-all; `set search_path=''` + schema-qualified; EXECUTE revoked from
public/anon/authenticated (the `0045` pattern). One enabled pilot (partial unique index); one active per connector; manual-only;
discovery-only; promotion-disabled; `schedule_allowed=false`; retention 1..90; ≤3 runs; composite same-tenant FK for isolation;
consent required before approval + re-checked at execution; deletion is plan+approve only (never auto-executes). Companion
connector-runner change adds the fail-closed pilot gate, permission/consent validator (NO customer token acquired — synthetic
fixtures), the reserve-before-run orchestrator, the manual-entrypoint customer-pilot mode + scheduled manual-only refusal, and a
mandatory-gate assertion (`runner_assert_not_pilot_governed`) so a customer connector cannot run off the ungated synthetic path.

**The plane activates nothing.** No pilot is enabled; no customer data/token/secret/Graph; no promotion; no schedule. Docs:
[`CONNECTOR_CUSTOMER_PILOT_MODEL.md`](./CONNECTOR_CUSTOMER_PILOT_MODEL.md), [`CONNECTOR_CUSTOMER_CONSENT_MODEL.md`](./CONNECTOR_CUSTOMER_CONSENT_MODEL.md),
[`CONNECTOR_PILOT_RETENTION_AND_DELETION.md`](./CONNECTOR_PILOT_RETENTION_AND_DELETION.md); evidence
[`evidence/P5E14_CUSTOMER_PILOT_CONTROL_PLANE_STAGING.md`](./evidence/P5E14_CUSTOMER_PILOT_CONTROL_PLANE_STAGING.md).

**Validated locally (GREEN):** migration-safety pass; the Docker RLS suite applies `0047` + runs `connector_customer_pilot_test.sql`
PL0–PL9 (all pass); connector-runner `tsc` + 733 tests + `vendor:verify` + `deploy:check` all green. **3-reviewer adversarial review**
(consent/isolation, execution-gating, privacy) — all confirmed findings fixed: one **P1** (customer-reference opacity) + five P2 +
one P3; everything else held. **Hosted-verified on staging (2026-07-14):** `0047` applied (sole new migration); hosted ACLs correct
(request roles hold 0 EXECUTE on the 16 pilot functions — Supabase default privileges did not reintroduce request-role execution;
connector_runner = exactly the 4 runner fns, 0 table DML); all 20 synthetic lifecycle proofs pass and roll back (zero fixtures
persist); the plane is dormant (0 enabled/runnable pilots, S2 schedule DISABLED, ECS 0/0/0); no secret/token/Graph/customer
execution. **Gate S3 = READY FOR ONE PILOT** (never PASS); enabling a real pilot is a further separate explicit GO. Entra stays
`certificationOnly`; **RISK-007 OPEN, Phase C BLOCKED**; staging only (`ycdpz…`); production (`dzbf…`) untouched.

## P5E16 — Okta Workforce Identity connector foundation (certification-only) · 2026-07-17

A provider-neutral, fail-closed **Okta** user-discovery connector FOUNDATION (in `idcaddie-connector-runner`) mirroring how Entra
began — but NOT copied: documented Okta-specific divergences (private_key_jwt auth vs client_secret; opaque ids; per-tenant
server-derived org host; bare-array response; RFC 5988 Link-header `after` cursor; string status enum; an Okta-specific strict
manifest the vendored framework schema can't express). 11 inert `okta-*` contract modules + a synthetic certification harness + 84
tests. **Okta is `certificationOnly`, disabled/future in the vendored provider-registry, and deliberately NOT registered in the
runner framework-registry — so `resolveFrameworkProvider("okta")` fails closed (`provider_not_registered`): non-runnable, not
customer-selectable, no live wiring/entrypoint/composition/deploy artifact, no manifest in the vendored (live) dir.** No DB
migration (`connectors.provider` + control-plane tables are provider-neutral). Reuses the neutral controls unchanged (http-safety
SSRF/exact-host guard, live/provider http clients, secret-provider, discovery-facts, db-writer). Auth: OAuth 2.0 service app with
private_key_jwt (least-privilege `okta.users.read`; SSWS legacy modeled as a secondary type). v1 scope: users only (groups/apps/
factors/logs deferred). Proven: credential isolation (staging ARN pinned to `/connector/okta/`; metadata-only doc parser — no
key/token leak; wrong tenant/connector/provider/version/status fail; cross-tenant + cross-provider token-cache bleed defense;
server-derived host; caller can't inject host/token/issuer/org/secret-ref); data minimization (no factor/MFA/credentials; unexpected
profile fields excluded; discovery-only facts `review_status=pending`; dedup; no raw payload; no promotion); deterministic
pagination + bounded retry; dormancy. `tsc` + **817 tests** + `vendor:verify` + `deploy:check` green. **No real Okta tenant/
credential/token/OAuth-token/API/ECS/schedule/promotion/production — none.** `microsoft_entra` unchanged; **RISK-007 OPEN, Phase C
BLOCKED**; synthetic-staging + certification only. Docs: [`OKTA_CONNECTOR_MODEL.md`](./OKTA_CONNECTOR_MODEL.md),
[`OKTA_CREDENTIAL_AND_CONSENT_MODEL.md`](./OKTA_CREDENTIAL_AND_CONSENT_MODEL.md), evidence
[`evidence/P5E16_OKTA_CONNECTOR_FOUNDATION.md`](./evidence/P5E16_OKTA_CONNECTOR_FOUNDATION.md) + connector-runner
`docs/OKTA_AUTHENTICATION_DESIGN.md`/`OKTA_CERTIFICATION_PLAN.md`/`OKTA_STAGING_OPERATIONS.md`/`docs/evidence/P5E16_OKTA_CONNECTOR_FOUNDATION.md`.

## P5E17 — customer connector experience (Okta-first, preview-only) · 2026-07-17

The customer-facing connector **experience** — marketplace → Okta detail → simulated connect wizard → preview management — so we can
see and refine exactly how a customer would connect an app, while every connector stays certification-only and non-runnable. New
`src/lib/customer-connectors/` centralizes the sole internal→customer mapping: `catalog.ts` (server-only; 12 curated providers —
Okta/Entra/Slack are "Preview", the other 8 "Coming soon", `scim_fixture` never surfaced; **`canSync`/`canSchedule` always false**;
**`canConnect` = Okta-only AND registry-not-ready** so a genuinely-runnable provider can never be shown as a mere preview),
`catalog-types.ts` (client-safe types + categories), `okta-content.ts` (customer copy + **SSRF-safe `validateOktaOrgHost`, no
network** — rejects non-Okta domains, schemes, credentials, ports, paths, localhost/internal, IPv4/IPv6), `demo-store.ts`
(**`sessionStorage`-only** preview state under one isolated key — never a DB/connector/credential/OAuth/token/secret/ECS/schedule

## Okta Discovery Phase 13 — effective-access graph engine (v3, compute-only)
A pure, provider-neutral, **compute-only** effective-access resolver at `src/lib/server/access-graph/` (`types.ts` + `resolve.ts` + `resolve.test.ts`) — no migration, no DB read, no RPC, no table, no fact, no write, no endpoint, no hosted run. Given the durable canonical edges passed in as typed row-id tuples (identities + `directory_group_memberships` + `directory_application_user_assignments` + `directory_application_group_assignments`, each carrying its `(tenant,connection,provider)` scope + provenance), it computes for every identity its DIRECT (`identity→app`), GROUP-derived (`identity→group→app`), and EFFECTIVE application access, classifying each reachable app **DIRECT / GROUP / BOTH**, deduping so each app appears once, and returning the full reasoning path (the direct edge's provenance + every group path's membership + assignment provenance). `resolveEffectiveAccess(graph, identityId, policy?)` + `resolveAllEffectiveAccess(graph, policy?)`. Reachability is a bounded 2-level DAG (memberships are identity↔group only — no group→group edge, so **cycles are structurally impossible**); algorithm is index-then-resolve **O(V+E)** with hash-set dedup (verified linear at 1000×1000 + a dense 200×50 fan-out, ~19ms). Isolation is honored by keying every index/lookup on `(scope, row-id)` (NUL-delimited, injective) so a cross-tenant / cross-connection / wrong-provider edge is filed under a different key and can never contribute; reachable apps are always `directory_applications` ROW ids, never `external_id`. Stale edges are included by default (a stale edge still asserts a relationship; the GO forbids inferring absent edges) with an optional `includeStale:false` current-only filter; "missing group" (a membership to a group with zero assignments) contributes nothing without erroring; the engine never enumerates the app table (never infers absent access). 24 tests (direct/group/both/multiple-groups/duplicate-paths/missing-group/never-infer/cross-tenant/connection/provider/cycle-termination/stale-policy/provenance/whole-tenant/unknown-identity/1000×1000/dense-fan-out). 10-lens adversarial review: 0 confirmed findings (1 refuted P3 provenance nit). No persistence, no analytics, no dashboards — those are later phases (Phase 14 = governance analytics). Additive; activates nothing. **No new risk opened/closed — RISK-007 OPEN, Phase C BLOCKED, staging only, production untouched.**

## Okta Discovery Phase 14 — governance-analytics engine (v3, compute-only)
A pure, provider-neutral, **compute-only** governance-analytics engine at `src/lib/server/governance-analytics/` (`types.ts` + `finding-id.ts` + `evaluate.ts` + `evaluate.test.ts`) — no migration, table, fact, RPC, route, component, dashboard, hosted task, network, DB read, `Date.now`, env, flag, or log. It consumes the canonical `directory_*` graph (identity/group/application nodes + membership/user-assignment/group-assignment edges, each carrying its `(tenant,connection,provider)` scope + `syncStatus`/provenance) + the Phase-13 effective-access results (computed in-engine) + an explicit policy, and produces **immutable, deterministic** `GovernanceFinding[]` + a counts-only summary. `evaluateGovernance(graph, policy?, ctx?)` + `evaluateIdentityGovernance` + `summarizeGovernance`. It builds only on `directory_*` + Phase 13 — **never** the legacy `app_users` model. Dependency direction is one-way: canonical graph → access-graph (Phase 13) → governance-analytics.

**Rule catalog (the GO's 10 families):** `redundant_direct_access` (medium/high), `identity_without_effective_access` (info/high), `group_without_application_reach` (info/high), `application_without_effective_identities` (low/high), `direct_assignment_with_stale_endpoint` + `group_assignment_with_stale_endpoint` (medium/high), `stale_only_effective_access` (medium/medium), `identity_broad_access` + `group_broad_application_reach` (threshold-gated, disabled by default), `duplicate_inherited_access_paths` (low/high), and structural diagnostics (`*_missing_*` high, `cross_scope`/`wrong_provider` medium — aggregate counts only, **no foreign id emitted**). Severity `info|low|medium|high` (never `critical` from topology); confidence `high|medium|low` (separate enum).

**Truthfulness boundary (docs/71):** the engine asserts **access topology only** — it never claims unused licenses, cost, savings, actual usage, inactivity, last-login, orphaned subs, shadow IT, or compliance (no such evidence exists in the graph). **Deterministic IDs:** sha256 over a byte-length-tagged, domain-prefixed serialization of `(ruleId, scope triple, subjectType, subjectId, sorted relatedIds)` — injective, scope-folded (isolation), order-independent, label/external-id-free; same input+policy → byte-identical output (findings sorted higher-severity-first). **Privacy:** findings carry only ROW ids + integer counts + bounded `syncStatus`/`staleSince` + message keys + injected `detectedAt`; the `scope` field is normalized to the bare triple so no edge id/PII leaks; structural findings emit no foreign id. **Current-vs-stale** separated (current-only primary view by default; `includeStale` opt-in; `stale_only` compares current-vs-all; a current path suppresses a stale-only finding). Index-then-resolve **O(V+E)**; verified at 1000×1000 nodes + 10000×10000 edges. Pure functions; the input graph is never mutated. 22 tests; 12-lens adversarial review. NO persistence/route/UI/hosted-run (Phase 15 productizes). Additive; activates nothing. **No new risk opened/closed — RISK-007 OPEN, Phase C BLOCKED, staging only, production untouched.**

## Okta Discovery Phase 15 Part 1 PR A — canonical directory product read RPCs (v3)
Migration `0061` + pgTAP + a test-harness fix only (no app code, no route, no hosted run). The FIRST reviewed customer read path onto the canonical directory graph, via **9 authenticated SECURITY DEFINER read RPCs** (read-access design Option B). The six canonical tables STAY deny-all (no SELECT policy, no table grant to authenticated); every read goes through the RPCs, each deriving the caller from `auth.uid()`, verifying tenant access via `has_tenant_role(p_tenant_id, {owner,admin})` (owner/admin only — ordinary membership is not sufficient to enumerate the whole access graph; a passed tenant_id is verified never trusted; viewer/editor/non-member/anon get a not-found-equivalent empty/null), pinning `search_path`, using no dynamic SQL, returning only bounded safe fields (row-id UUIDs + safe display columns + sync_status/stale_since) and NEVER external_id/raw_payload/credentials/settings/profiles/secret-metadata, paginating deterministically (order by id, cursor, page size capped 100), and making foreign-tenant/missing ids return the same not-found. Inventory: counts; three node lists + three edge lists (bounded/paginated); two entity-focused subgraphs (identity, application). EXECUTE granted to authenticated only; public/anon denied; connector_runner write path untouched. Also fixes the `scripts/test-rls.sh` masking gap (the re-revoke list now includes the five new `directory_*` tables so the suite reflects their real deny-all posture). Tested in `access_product_read_rpcs_test.sql` (Docker pgTAP: owner/admin read; editor/viewer/non-member/anon denied; tenant isolation; foreign/missing = not-found; no external_id leak; pagination cap; stale policy) — green with all okta + org RLS suites. Additive; activates nothing; no route/UI/hosted-run (PR B adds the server loader + UI). **No new risk opened/closed — RISK-007 OPEN, Phase C BLOCKED, staging only, production untouched.**

## Okta Discovery Phase 15 Part 1 PR B — access product surface (server repo + view models + /access UI) (v3)
The first reviewed customer-facing consumer of the canonical directory access graph — a read-only `/access` route group (overview, findings, identity detail, application detail) that explains DIRECT/GROUP/BOTH effective access and surfaces governance findings. No migration, no browser DB/RPC access, no mutation, no exports, no hosted run. Server-only repository (`src/lib/data/access-repository.ts`) is the ONLY RPC caller (the 0061 authenticated SECURITY DEFINER RPCs); it uses the user-scoped RLS-governed server client (never service-role), resolves the active tenant via trusted `resolveTenantContext`, gates owner/admin, and the RPC re-verifies via `has_tenant_role`. `access-rpc-types.ts` validates every RPC response with strict zod (strips prohibited keys, drops malformed rows). `access-graph-assembly.ts` maps rows into the Phase-14 GovernanceGraph, injecting the verified tenant into every scope. The loaders REUSE Phase-13 `resolveEffectiveAccess`/`resolveAllEffectiveAccess` + Phase-14 `evaluateGovernance`/`evaluateIdentityGovernance` (never reimplemented; `detectedAt` injected). Browser-safe view models carry only safe display labels + integer counts + the bounded `syncStatus` enum + row-id UUIDs used solely as href params — NEVER external_id/raw_payload/login/email/credentials/settings/profiles/source_endpoint/last_discovery_run_id/secrets/foreign tenant id. Exhaustive `governance-presenter.ts` maps all 17 rule ids to truthful copy (no usage/license/cost/savings/inactivity/compliance/over-provisioning/safe-removal claims; severity never "critical"). Owner/admin only (editor/viewer/non-member/anon denied); foreign/missing/denied entity ids render an identical inline "Not found" (no existence disclosure). Overview gates the whole-graph evaluation behind a counts check (nodes ≤2,000 / edges ≤5,000) + bounded paging (cap 100), failing closed with a truthful "too large to evaluate" state — never a silent truncation or a false "no findings". Caching: cookies force per-request dynamic; no `unstable_cache`/`use cache`/module memo around per-tenant reads; no API route. `/access` registered in nav (Insights). Also fixes the `test-rls.sh` masking gap already noted in PR A. Docs: `docs/72_ACCESS_SURFACE_DESIGN.md`. Tests (39 new): governance-presenter exhaustive+forbidden-term scan; graph-assembly tenant-injection + valid-engine-input + zod drop/strip; view-model label fallback + finding mapper + privacy + classification-carried-verbatim-from-engine; repository static-safety (no service-role/no writes/no direct table query/RPC-only); route UI (forbidden/complete/too_large/not-found-indistinguishable/no-mutation/UUID-only-in-href/bounded-detail-banner/one-h1-per-state/aria-current-filter); extended ui-regression (stricter ACCESS_PAGES) + route-boundaries (3 access error boundaries). tsc + full suite (1575) + eslint + docs/auth-safety/app-runtime-imports/migration-safety gates + Next build green.

**14-lens adversarial review (find→skeptic-verify):** no P0/P1. 5 confirmed findings fixed before merge, each with a regression test: (P2) identity/application detail loaders were unbounded — a fan-in-heavy app (e.g. an "all employees" group) bypassed the overview's fail-closed cap; now capped at `SUBGRAPH_MAX_ROWS = 5000` total subgraph rows, returning a `bounded: true` flag that renders a truthful "too large to display in full" banner instead of resolving + `.map()`-ing an unbounded list. (P3) DIRECT/GROUP/BOTH was re-derived from `direct`/`groupPaths` in the loader — now sourced verbatim from Phase-13 `AppAccess.classification` (single source of truth); the renamed `classificationView(classification, groupCount)` only renders it. (P3) two view-model comments falsely said login/email are NEVER label fallbacks — corrected (they ARE permitted owner/admin fallbacks per docs/72). (P3) detail-page not-found/error states rendered no `<h1>` — every state now exposes exactly one. (P3) the active severity-filter pill was color-only — now also `aria-current="page"`. 1 finding refuted (identityLabel login/email as visible text is intended per docs/72). **No new risk opened/closed — RISK-007 OPEN, Phase C BLOCKED, staging only, production untouched.**

## Okta Discovery Phase 15 Part 2 PR C — access surface search, filtering, pagination, drill-down, evidence UX (v3)
Completes the read-only `/access` experience with server-side search + filtering + deterministic pagination + finding drill-down + preserved navigation context. **No migration, no browser DB/RPC access, no mutation, no exports, no hosted run.** New pure module `access-filters.ts` is the single strict filter/pagination/return-context contract: it parses ONLY allowlisted values off `searchParams` (query normalized NFKC+lowercase+≤200 chars; severity/confidence/classification/subjectType/catalogMatch/ruleId allowlist-guarded; provider `^[a-z0-9_]{1,40}$`; connectionId UUID; page ≥1; pageSize clamped [1,100] default 50), takes the first value of a repeated param and ignores unknown params (no scope widening), and serializes canonical URLs (fixed key order, defaults omitted, filter change resets to page 1). **Filtering runs server-side over the ALREADY-EVALUATED safe view models — after Phase 13/14, never over raw canonical rows and never before resolution — so filters can never change effective-access or finding meaning.** Filtered totals show ONLY when evaluation is `complete` (bounded/`too_large` keeps the truthful banner; never a false "no findings"). **Pagination** is deterministic in-memory OFFSET over the already-bounded lists (overview node/edge caps bound total findings; `SUBGRAPH_MAX_ROWS` bounds detail rows) — findings keep the engine's severity-first order, detail lists sort by label then canonical id; no cursor (data is fully materialized + bounded). **Finding drill-down** is a native `<details>/<summary>` (zero client JS, natively accessible) revealing summary/guidance/scope/structured evidence/subject link/disclaimer — no finding-detail route, no persistence, no finding-id enumeration surface. **Return context** is an allowlisted contract (`from` ∈ {overview,findings,identity,application}; `fromId` must be a UUID; `ret` is a bounded querystring re-parsed through the same allowlist parser) — `backLink` only ever emits a fixed `/access/...` path, so a hostile `from`/`ret`/`fromId` (absolute/protocol-relative/`javascript:`/traversal/oversized) falls back to the static back link and open redirect is structurally impossible. Two safe bounded enums (`ruleId`, `subjectType`) added to `GovernanceFindingView` for the rule/subject filters (never a foreign id/PII). Group-path + assigned-group lists are bounded in the browser (`+N more`). Overview adds severity filter-links + a findings search shortcut (counts only — no risk/savings/utilization score). Accessibility: native labelled GET forms (no JS for core function), `aria-current="page"` pagination, `role="status"` banners. Docs: `docs/72` Part 2 section. Tests (+31, total 1606): `access-filters.test.ts` (allowlist parsing, canonical serialization, offset pagination no-drop/no-dup, filter predicates, deterministic sort, per-surface active-filter counts, **open-redirect refutation** for backLink, round-trip) + extended `access.ui.test.tsx` (filter form, severity/search narrowing, pagination, `<details>` drill-down, distinct empty states, return-context links, hostile `from` fallback, detail filters, stale-evidence marker, capped detail findings). tsc + full suite (1606) + eslint + docs/auth-safety/app-runtime-imports/migration-safety gates + Next build green.

**18-lens adversarial review (3 skeptic passes: security/redirect/scope, correctness/completeness, truthfulness/privacy/a11y):** no P0/P1. Confirmed findings fixed before merge, each with a regression test: (P2) `activeFilterCount` counted filters a surface doesn't apply, so a complete-empty scope reached with an unapplied param (e.g. `/access/findings?classification=DIRECT`) mislabeled the empty state as "no matches" — replaced with **per-surface** `findingsActiveFilters`/`detailActiveFilters` counting only applied fields (deferred provider/connection/catalogMatch never counted). (P2) the application-detail identities table had no per-row stale marker (asymmetric with the identity page) — added `staleEvidence` to `ApplicationIdentityAccessView` + loader + a "Stale evidence" badge + a staleEvidence filter. (P2) detail-page findings lists rendered uncapped (bounded only by `SUBGRAPH_MAX_ROWS`) — capped at 50 with a truthful "Showing the first 50 of N" note. (P3) pagination `<nav>` gained `flex-wrap`; load-error `<p>`s gained `role="alert"`. Refuted/no-op: backLink open-redirect (structurally impossible — fixed `/access` targets, `ret` re-parsed through the allowlist, `fromId` must be a UUID); login/email as visible search/label text (documented, intended owner/admin fallback per docs/72); pagination enabled/disabled arrows (mitigated — disabled is a non-focusable `<span>`). **No new risk opened/closed — RISK-007 OPEN, Phase C BLOCKED, staging only, production untouched.**

## Okta Discovery Phase 15 Part 2 PR D — bounded CSV exports (v3)
Read-only CSV export for the /access surface via **three authenticated GET route handlers** — `/access/findings/export`, `/access/identities/[id]/export`, `/access/applications/[id]/export`. **No migration, no mutation, no service-role, no browser RPC, no storage bucket, no persisted file, no background job, no email, no hosted run.** Each route reuses the SAME server-only loader as its page (owner/admin gate via `accessGate` → the 0061 RPCs; canonical tables stay deny-all) and the SAME `parseAccessFilters` + filter functions, so **export authorization and contents are identical to the page**. `forbidden → 403`; a foreign/missing/unauthorized entity id → the SAME `404` as a real one (no existence disclosure). **Exports only from complete evaluations** — findings require overview `status==="complete"` (else `409`), detail routes refuse when the subgraph is `bounded` (`409`) — and **refuse (never truncate) above `EXPORT_ROW_CAP=10,000`** filtered rows (`413`). New `sanitizeCsvCell` in `to-csv.ts` (additive; existing client exports unchanged) prefixes a single quote when a cell begins — after optional whitespace — with a formula/command trigger (`= + - @`) or a control char (TAB/CR/LF), neutralizing spreadsheet formula/DDE injection; `toCsv` then RFC-4180-quotes commas/quotes/newlines. `access-export.ts` projects the safe view models onto an **explicit column allowlist** (findings: finding_id/severity/confidence/finding_type/title/summary/subject_type/subject_label/stale_evidence/evidence_summary/review_guidance; identity + application access: labels/provider/classification/direct_assignment_count/[inherited_group_count/labels]/stale_evidence) and sanitizes every cell + header. NEVER emits a tenant/canonical/external id, raw payload, credential, setting, profile, source endpoint, secret, raw JSON evidence, or a separate login/email column (subject/identity/application values are the same resolved display labels shown on-screen; `finding_id` is the Phase-14 deterministic governance hash, not a canonical row UUID). Responses: `text/csv; charset=utf-8` + `Content-Disposition: attachment` + `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff` + `Cache-Control: no-store, private`; filenames are a fixed prefix + date only (`access-findings-YYYY-MM-DD.csv`). The pages show an "Export CSV" link (carrying current filters) only when there are rows. Docs: `docs/72` PR D section. Tests (+24, total 1630): `to-csv.test.ts` (formula-injection neutralization), `access-export.test.ts` (column allowlists, sanitize+quote interplay, no-uuid/no-external-id output, filenames, response headers, cap constant), `access-export-routes.test.ts` (auth parity 403/404-indistinguishable/503, complete-only 409, 10k-cap 413, filter application, private/no-store/nosniff/attachment headers, no CSV on error).

**Adversarial review (workflow: 5 dimensions — csv-injection, authz-parity, complete-cap, privacy-columns, response-method — each finding skeptic-verified):** no P0/P1/P2. 1 confirmed P3 + 3 refuted P3. **Confirmed (P3, accepted):** the `413` row-cap branch is unreachable on the two DETAIL routes (a detail subgraph is already capped at `SUBGRAPH_MAX_ROWS=5000 < 10,000`) — kept as correct, future-proof defense-in-depth (meaningful only on the findings export, and if `SUBGRAPH_MAX_ROWS` is ever raised). **Fixed:** `exportError` now also sets `nosniff` (uniform with `csvResponse`). **Refuted:** a `pageAll` silent-truncation misreporting "complete" (upstream PR B, unreachable — the counts gate caps lists at ≤5000 and list RPCs always return `id`); uncaught route exceptions leaking detail (Next's production 500 is digest-only, matching the existing route-handler convention). **No new risk opened/closed — RISK-007 OPEN, Phase C BLOCKED, staging only, production untouched.**

## Okta Discovery Phase 15 Part 2 PR E — staging verification script + a11y/responsive/diagnostics hardening (v3)
Guarded, read-only staging verifier for the `/access` surface + a11y regression suite + runbook. **No migration, no graph/governance/export-logic change, no product-UI change, no connector-runner/AWS/hosted-task/recurring-sync, no production.** `scripts/verify-staging-access-surface.mjs` follows the established `verify-staging-*.mjs` pattern with a dry-run testability seam: staging-ref hard guard (`ACCESS_SURFACE_REF_FILE` override; refuses production `dzbfxulvxchdemcettrx`), `--preflight` (guards + check plan + env presence, NO network/creds), fail-closed on missing env, anon-key/synthetic-user only (never a service-role/secret key), read-only calls restricted to an explicit **RPC allowlist** (the 9 migration-0061 read RPCs) + a GET **route allowlist**, and fully redacted output (no passwords/tokens/cookies/anon-key/external-ids/raw-RPC/labels/emails/ids — only check-id + PASS/FAIL + aggregate counts). Live checks (human-run, when creds present): unauthenticated `/access` route-deny; owner/admin allowed + editor/viewer/non-member/anon denied; count parity (identities 1/groups 2/apps 2/memberships 1/direct 1/group 0); known identity resolves DIRECT-only + both apps resolve; nonexistent/foreign id → not-found-equivalent `null`; privacy scan of every RPC response. Runbook `docs/73_ACCESS_STAGING_VERIFICATION.md` (docs/31-shaped): prerequisites, env var names + JSON shape, ref guard, synthetic-user roles, safe commands, preflight, expected counts + authz outcomes, privacy assertions, manual UI acceptance checklist, failure interpretation, read-only + production-prohibition statements. **Accessibility:** a review of the /access surface against the full checklist found it already compliant from PR C/D (one `<h1>` per page/state, labelled GET-form controls, `aria-current` pagination, `role=status|alert` live regions, native `<details>` drill-down, text-not-color meaning, `overflow-x-auto` tables, `flex-wrap` bars, bounded lists, no animation → reduced-motion N/A); PR E **locks it in** with a new `access-a11y.ui.test.tsx` regression suite (no product-code change required; no axe dep — asserted via testing-library role/name/label queries, the repo convention). **Operational diagnostics: DEFERRED** — the repo has no approved telemetry/observability abstraction (only ad-hoc fixed-string `console.error`); per the phase instruction no ad-hoc console logging was added (documented in docs/73). Tests (+35, total 1652): `verify-staging-access-surface.test.ts` (subprocess guard tests — missing ref/env fail-closed, production ref/URL/host refused, service-role JWT + `sb_secret_*` refused, spoofed-host DB URL refused, preflight, redaction; + source-import/no-mutation static assertions + `node --check`) and `access-a11y.ui.test.tsx`.

**Adversarial review (workflow: 4 dimensions — script-safety, doc-accuracy, a11y-suite, scope — each finding skeptic-verified):** no P0/P1. 9 confirmed (2 P2 + 7 P3), all fixed with regression tests; 1 refuted. **Fixed P2:** the service-role guard only caught legacy JWT keys → now `isAnonKey` accepts ONLY an anon JWT or `sb_publishable_*` and refuses `sb_secret_*`/service_role/unknown (fail closed); `STAGING_APP_URL` was not pinned to staging (a production frontend passed) → now parsed + a production-host denylist refuses it. **Fixed P3:** `STAGING_SUPABASE_URL` substring check was spoofable (`…supabase.co.attacker.example`) → now an exact-host match (`ycdpzduxugdsffjqyoai.supabase.co`); the O2 "owner JWT is user-scoped" check was hardcoded `true` → now decodes the owner session token; the a11y "labelled control" loop only checked DOM nesting (and included hidden inputs) → now asserts a non-empty accessible name and excludes `type=hidden`; the a11y findings test skipped the error state → added; runbook exit-code + "never printed" wording corrected (the non-secret app URL appears in the banner). **Refuted:** the overview/findings `<h1>` is outside every branch (always present). **No new risk opened/closed — RISK-007 OPEN, Phase C BLOCKED, staging only, production untouched.**

## Okta Discovery Phase 15 Part 2 PR F — isolated-v3 verifier mode (explicit allowlisted read-only target) (v3)
Refactors `scripts/verify-staging-access-surface.mjs` to support one explicitly reviewed, isolated V3 web deployment as a read-only validation target — **without weakening any global safeguard**. **No product code, no migration, no grant change, no graph/governance/export change, no connector-runner/AWS/hosted-task/recurring-sync, no production.** New `ACCESS_VERIFY_MODE` (explicit opt-in; **never inferred from the host**): `staging` (default; unchanged — `STAGING_APP_URL`, production/legacy hosts refused) or `isolated-v3` (app target `ACCESS_VERIFY_APP_URL`, host must EXACTLY match both the operator-supplied `ACCESS_VERIFY_ALLOWED_HOST` and a **statically reviewed allowlist** `ISOLATED_V3_ALLOWED_HOSTS = ["idcaddie-v3.vercel.app"]` — no wildcard/suffix/substring; adding a host is a reviewed code edit). **Every fail-closed safeguard is preserved in both modes:** the database target stays STAGING Supabase (`STAGING_SUPABASE_URL` host must be EXACTLY `ycdpzduxugdsffjqyoai.supabase.co`; production ref `dzbfxulvxchdemcettrx` refused), anon/publishable-key-only (legacy service-role JWT **and** `sb_secret_*` refused), read-only RPC + GET route allowlists, redacted output, fail-closed on missing env. App URLs are parsed as strict bare https origins (no credentials/path/query/fragment; the Supabase URL too). Ambiguous config is refused (`ACCESS_VERIFY_APP_URL` in staging mode, or a conflicting `STAGING_APP_URL` in isolated-v3 mode). Output + preflight are mode-labelled ("Verification target: isolated V3 web deployment; staging Supabase; read-only" + target-type/database/operation/connector-mutation/production-rejected/legacy-not-targeted lines). `STAGING_APP_URL` retained as the staging-mode alias (backward compatible). Runbook `docs/73` updated: modes, why isolated-v3 exists (Vercel "Production" = deployment channel, not data authorization), isolated-v3 invocation, and an explicit **sunset condition** (remove/re-review before V3 becomes customer-facing / connects to production Supabase / gets real data / enables live connectors / Phase C unblocks / RISK-007 closes / provider mutation authorized). Tests (`verify-staging-access-surface.test.ts`, 34 total): mode selection (default/empty→staging, explicit isolated-v3, unknown rejected, not-inferred-from-host), isolated-v3 host allowlist (exact host accepted; lookalike/suffix-spoof, allowed-host mismatch, http, URL-credentials, path, explicit-port all rejected; legacy production host rejected), isolated-v3 live guards (requires app-url + allowed-host, ambiguous config rejected, production Supabase + service-role/`sb_secret_*` still rejected), staging-mode ambiguity, and strengthened source-safety (RPC allowlist is EXACTLY the 9 read RPCs; the only DB egress is `client.rpc(...)` in the wrapper + no direct `.from("` table query; no dynamic import/require). Backward-compat: all prior staging-mode guard tests remain green.

**Adversarial review (workflow: 8 lenses — mode-confusion, host-allowlist, supabase-isolation, credential-type, read-only, redaction, backward-compat, sunset — each finding skeptic-verified):** no P0/P1. 11 confirmed (2 P2 + 9 P3), fixed; 4 refuted. **Fixed P2 (test-hardening — runtime was already safe):** the static "no mutation" test only grepped PostgREST verbs (couldn't catch a mutation RPC) → added an assertion that `RPC_ALLOWLIST` is EXACTLY the 9 read RPCs; the RPC allowlist was enforced only at runtime → added a static assertion that `client.rpc(...)` in the wrapper is the sole DB egress (no other `.rpc(`, no `.from("`). **Fixed P3:** `httpsBaseHost` now rejects an explicit port (bare-origin intent explicit; `:443` still normalizes away); the ref-mismatch and unknown-mode `die()` messages no longer echo the env/file-derived value (redaction); the unreachable-app-URL `die()` is now a static message + the app fetch is explicit `method:"GET"`; docs corrected (staging ambiguity is refused only on a *conflicting* value; exit-2 lists "unreachable app URL" not "staging app URL"); no-dynamic-import test added. **Accepted (non-refuting caveats, no change):** staging mode keeps its denylist (not an allowlist) per "preserve existing behavior" — it accepts non-production hosts, but only empty-cookie GETs reach the app host (no credential), and isolated-v3 is the strict path; `ACCESS_VERIFY_MODE` is whitespace-trimmed (still an explicit env opt-in, case-sensitive); the staging app URL validator was tightened to a bare origin (intentional hardening). **Refuted:** anon-key project-ref not validated (key still fails auth against the exact-host-pinned staging URL); network-level DNS redirection (out of scope; DB URL exact-host + `redirect:"manual"`); GET-only "incidental" (now explicit); top-level catch prints `e.message` (system/network errors, not secrets — matches the existing verify-staging convention).

## Okta Discovery Phase 15 Part 2 PR G — verifier owner/admin optional + partial-run semantics (v3)
Staging-fixture inspection found the canonical tenant `aaaa1111-1111-1111-1111-111111111111` has **only `editor` + `viewer`** memberships — **no owner, no admin** — so the previous verifier (which *required* an owner) could not run at all against the real fixture. **Scripts/docs only; no product code, no migration, no grant change, no connector-runner/AWS/hosted-task, no production.** Fix (chosen over weakening the verifier or inventing an owner): make **owner/admin OPTIONAL and validated-only-if-supplied** (like editor/viewer/nonMember); `expectedTenantId` is now the sole required field. The **positive path** (O1–O7 + A1: counts parity, entity resolution, DIRECT classification, the privacy scan of the *returned* payload, authorized export) uses **owner ∨ admin** as the authorized reader — because `/access` is **owner/admin-only by design** (the 0061 RPCs gate on `has_tenant_role({owner,admin})` and return `null` to everyone else, so no unauthorized principal can read the positive data). With **neither** present the run is **PARTIAL**: a distinct `SKIP` state (never counted as passed) is recorded for the `POS` line, `finish()` prints a loud `PARTIAL RUN` banner and an `N passed / K skipped / 0 failed` summary, and the **full deny boundary still runs** (U1–U3 unauthenticated route-deny; D1/D2/D3 editor/viewer/non-member denied; N1 anon denied) + structure. A supplied-but-malformed principal is still refused; a configured principal that fails to sign in is a `FAIL` (not a skip). **Why an owner/admin fixture is genuinely needed for full coverage** (not verifier strictness): the positive checks require an authorized reader; the documented **minimum** synthetic data to add is ONE synthetic Auth user + ONE `tenant_memberships` row (`role='owner'`|`'admin'`, active) in that tenant — no schema/grant/directory change. docs/73 updated (owner/admin optional, partial-run semantics, minimum-fixture, exit-code note incl. SKIP). Tests (`verify-staging-access-surface.test.ts`, 39 total): editor/viewer-only fixture passes the guards + reaches the live run (owner optional); supplied-but-malformed owner/editor rejected; type-checked (non-string email refused); `expectedTenantId` still required. `node --check` + full suite + tsc + eslint + gates + Next build green.

**Adversarial review (workflow: 6 lenses — skip-strictness, positive-gate, deny-still-runs, validate-if-supplied, preserved-safety, backward-compat — each finding skeptic-verified):** no P0. 17 confirmed (1 P1 + several P2/P3), fixed; 5 refuted. **The central finding (P1/P2), now fixed:** a PARTIAL run (no owner/admin) has **no positive control**, so the deny checks alone cannot distinguish a correctly-denying surface from one that denies EVERYONE (0061 RPCs unapplied/broken) — the previous banner over-claimed "verified the deny boundary" and exited 0. Now: `finish()` prints an honest **INCONCLUSIVE (PARTIAL)** banner (deny-only evidence is not proof the surface works) and **exits 3** (distinct from full-pass 0 / fail 1 / guard 2); and the deny checks require a **clean** deny — `null` data **and** no RPC error — so a missing/broken RPC is a `FAIL`, not a false "denied". **Other fixes:** the skip banner was POS-only → role-deny skips (D1/D2/D3) are now surfaced too; `U1–U3` → `U1–U7` (all seven routes incl. `:id` + `/export`); owner-sign-in-fail id `O1`→`O0` (no duplicate id vs the positive `O1`); `A1` skip detail distinguishes "no admin" / "admin sign-in failed" / "only admin (no owner)"; guard-4 principal validation is type-checked (non-string email/password refused); `O5/O6` are `SKIP`'d (not silently dropped) on cardinality drift; docs/73 corrected (partial run is inconclusive not "verified"; exit-3; O0/A0/U1–U7 documented; minimum-fixture wording). **Accepted (documented) limitation:** the deny checks exercise `product_directory_access_counts` as the representative RPC (all nine 0061 RPCs share the same `has_tenant_role` gate) rather than sweeping all nine — a deliberate scope choice, not a leak. **Refuted:** anon/DNS-level concerns and the reassuring-tail-line nit (the honest INCONCLUSIVE banner + exit 3 supersede it). No product code / migration / grant / connector-runner / AWS / production change.

## Okta Discovery Phase 15 Part 2 PR H — verifier O5 (DIRECT-only classification) + N1 (anon permission-denied) fixes (v3)
The first live isolated-v3 run (owner fixture added; exit 1) surfaced **two verifier-only bugs** — the product is correct in both cases. **Scripts/docs only; no product logic, migration, grant, RLS, connector-runner, AWS, hosted-task, or production change.** **O5 (root cause — verifier assertion bug):** the DIRECT-only check required raw `memberships === 0`, but the migration-0061 identity subgraph scopes `groupAssignments` to the identity's OWN groups (`directory_group_id = any(v_group_ids)`), so `groupAssignments.length` IS the inherited-application-path count — a **bare group membership with no group→app assignment is not an inherited path**. The staging fixture (1 direct assignment, 1 membership, **0** group→app assignments) is correctly **DIRECT** in Phase 13; the assertion was wrong. Fixed: O5 now asserts effective-access semantics via a pure `classifyIdentitySubgraph` — `classification === "DIRECT" && directCount === 1 && inheritedPathCount === 0` (membership count is irrelevant to classification). **N1 (root cause — verifier expectation bug):** 0061 `revoke execute … from public, anon, authenticated` then grants EXECUTE to `authenticated` only, so **anon lacks EXECUTE** and its *legitimate* denial is a permission-denied — **SQLSTATE `42501`** (Postgres insufficient_privilege), NOT a clean null; PR G's `error == null` requirement wrongly failed it. Fixed: N1 now accepts a **narrowly allowlisted** anon denial via a pure `recognizedAnonDeny` — null data with **no error OR `error.code === "42501"`** — and still FAILs for returned data (leak) or any other error (function-not-found `42883`, schema-cache, malformed, server, timeout); only the SQLSTATE **code** is logged, never the message (matching the repo's `supabase-slack-resolver-store.ts:39` convention). **D1/D2/D3 unchanged** — editor/viewer/non-member are `authenticated`, hold EXECUTE, reach the function body, and get a clean null (source-proven; an error there stays a `FAIL`). New pure lib `scripts/access-verify-lib.mjs` (no I/O, unit-tested). Tests (+11, `access-verify-lib.test.ts`): DIRECT-only (direct + unrelated membership + 0 group→app), GROUP (actual group path), BOTH (direct + inherited), NONE, null-safe; anon 42501 = clean deny, arbitrary error / function-not-found / returned-data = fail, reason logs code-only. `node --check` + full suite (1687) + tsc + eslint + gates + Next build green.

**Adversarial review (workflow: 5 lenses — o5-classification, n1-allowlist, redaction, no-weakening, scope-product — each finding skeptic-verified):** no P0/P1 — both fixes are confirmed correct (no product defect masked; `recognizedAnonDeny` sound; the deny boundary is not weakened; D1/D2/D3 unchanged; redaction holds — code-only). 5 confirmed P2/P3, all coverage/doc: **fixed** — N1 now sweeps **all nine** 0061 RPCs for anon (a per-function anon mis-grant would otherwise slip through) with correct per-RPC args so PostgREST resolves the function before the EXECUTE-deny; docs/73 stale duplicate `D1–D3`/`N1` table rows removed; the manual UI checklist now asserts the identity's **"Direct" badge** (the end-to-end render O5 verifies only at the RPC layer). **Accepted + documented limitation:** against the current fixture (0 group→app assignments) O5 live-covers only the DIRECT branch — a false GROUP/BOTH or an over-broad subgraph-scoping bug is undetectable without directory data (out of scope: no directory change); GROUP/BOTH are covered by the unit tests + the manual badge check, and O5 is an RPC-level structural oracle (not the Phase-13 classifier or the rendered badge). **Refuted:** `recognizedAnonDeny(undefined, …)` (the wrapper always returns `{data:null|value, error}`, never `undefined`); O3-counts-all vs O5-identity-scoped measuring different things (both 0 for this fixture — consistent); inheritedPathCount row-vs-distinct-app (row>0 ⟺ a path exists — correct for classification). **No new risk opened/closed — RISK-007 OPEN, Phase C BLOCKED, staging Supabase only, production untouched.**

## Okta Discovery Phase 15 Part 2 PR I — fix /access load failure (counts RPC arg mismatch) (v3)
**Manual-acceptance blocker:** the authorized owner opening `/access` on the isolated-v3 deployment got the generic "Access data could not be loaded" — even though the direct staging verifier passed conclusively (owner allowed, counts 1/2/2/1/1/0, editor/viewer/anon denied). **Root cause — an application RPC-argument mismatch (app code, not deployment/data/grant):** `access-repository.ts` `getAccessCounts` called `product_directory_access_counts` with `{ p_tenant_id, p_include_stale }`, but the migration-0061 counts function declares only `(p_tenant_id, p_connection_id default null, p_provider default null)` — it has **no `p_include_stale`**. PostgREST resolves an RPC by matching the JSON body keys to a function overload, so the extra arg matched none → **PGRST202 "could not find the function … in the schema cache"** → `callRpc` → `query_failed` → `loadAccessOverview` → the safe inline error. The verifier passed because it (and the loaders'/repository's mocked unit tests) called the counts RPC with `{ p_tenant_id }` only — the app's real arg set was never exercised against the real 0061 signatures. **Fix (smallest correct):** drop `p_include_stale` from the counts RPC call (the counts function is stale-AGNOSTIC — it counts all rows, the correct conservative bound for the overview's too-large gate) and the now-unused `includeStale` param from `getAccessCounts` + its one loader call site. The list + subgraph RPC calls already match their signatures (they legitimately declare `p_include_stale`/`p_after_id`/`p_limit`). **No migration / grant / RLS / role / fixture / connector-runner / AWS / production change; no validation weakened; no service-role; no direct canonical-table query; no raw error exposed.** Regression test (`access-rpc-args.test.ts`): captures every accessor's RPC args via a mocked server client and asserts, for all nine accessors, both directions of the arg-mismatch class — each arg key ⊆ the 0061 function's declared params AND every **required** (no-default) param is present (a dropped `p_tenant_id`/`p_identity_id` yields the same PGRST202) — plus that `getAccessCounts` passes exactly `{ p_tenant_id }`; proven to fail on the pre-fix code and pass after.

**Adversarial review (workflow: 4 lenses — fix-correct-complete, completeness-gate, full-path-shape, regression-soundness — each finding skeptic-verified):** no P0/P1. **`full-path-shape` + `fix-correct-complete` found nothing → the fix fully resolves the page** (no downstream response-shape mismatch; every other list/subgraph RPC arg already matches its signature). 4 confirmed P2/P3, addressed: (P2) the complete-view StatCards would show the stale-agnostic RPC **total** while the body is current-only → the displayed complete-view counts now come from the **paged rows** (which honor `includeStale`), matching the evaluated graph exactly, with a new `access-loaders.test.ts` (real assembly + engines, mocked repository) proving it; the too-large gate + too_large-view counts keep the RPC total (conservative, accurate). (P2) the regression test only checked the extra-arg direction → now also asserts required params present. (P3) `too_large` uses the total count, so a stale-heavy tenant can over-block the default current-only view (fail-closed safe; a stale-aware pre-count needs a migration) — documented in `docs/72`. (P3) the args test hand-enumerates the nine accessors — a new accessor needs its own call (noted). **Refuted:** the too_large-view "counts accurate" copy (true — total-directory counts in that mode); `declaredParams` reading source not schema-cache (source is the source of truth for the code-vs-signature bug class). tsc + full suite (1692) + eslint + docs/auth-safety/app-runtime-imports/migration-safety/no-real-tokens gates + Next build green. **No new risk opened/closed — RISK-007 OPEN, Phase C BLOCKED, staging Supabase only, production untouched.** After merge, the manual review restarts at `/access` (next idcaddie-v3.vercel.app deploy auto-builds from main).

## Phase 8D — canonical SaaS evidence write boundary (migration 0077 + runner Part B)
0076 built the destination tables for SaaS account evidence; **nothing could write to them**. 8D is the door: five runner-only `SECURITY DEFINER` RPCs, a per-resource completeness table, a stale-transition audit trigger, and the runner-side adapters that use them. **Scope held: the customer OAuth callback is untouched (still synthetic), no Slack contact, no ECS task, no scheduling, no production, no Slack-specific tables, nothing written into `identity_accounts`.** Full model in **docs/82**.

**The load-bearing decision — `connector_run_resource_discovery` keyed `(run_id, resource)`.** `connector_run_discovery` is `PRIMARY KEY (run_id)`: one completeness row per run, **no resource discriminator**. Correct when a run read one resource; a Slack sweep reads two, so a complete `usergroups.list` plus a half-dead `users.list` would authorize staling every account the user sweep never reached — a partial read rendering as a workspace that emptied out. A new per-resource table was chosen over altering the proven Okta gate, which is untouched. `resource` is the emitted **fact type**, not an endpoint id (two endpoints could answer for the same resource, and it removes a runner-side mapping that could drift from the manifest). Test W4 is the argument in executable form. Eligibility is unchanged otherwise: `completeness ∧ rejected=0 ∧ termination='last_page' ∧ ¬review_required`, plus latest-run guard, connector `for update` lock, first-run rule, and the same 30%/100-row circuit breaker; a refusal returns as **data** (`eligible`/`superseded`/`circuitBreakerTriggered`), not an exception. Promotion pins the provider to the **connector** (never the fact), reads only named fields, and always sets `sync_status='current'` **and** `stale_since=null` (the 0070 invariant, applied from the start). Stale rows are retained, never deleted.

**Two real defects found and fixed, both pre-existing.** (1) `app_user_account` and `group` were the only allowlisted fact types with **no key allowlist** — they had no promoter, so nothing read them; both live paths (`live.ts`, `microsoft-entra-live.ts`) were passing the executor's whole flat fact as `fact_json`, duplicating the envelope inside the payload. 0077 adds the allowlists and the runner now splits envelope→columns / payload→`fact_json` (`saas-fact-rows.ts`). The allowlist is deliberately **narrower** than the fact schema (`app_id_hint`, `group_type`, `role_hint`, `source_user_id`, `last_activity_at` have nowhere to land in 0076) and **refuses** rather than filters — a dropped key is indistinguishable from a key that was never emitted. (2) fact persistence was one connection acquire + one transaction **per fact**; now batched at 500/transaction.

**Bounded vocabulary derived in exactly one place.** The fact carries provider **observations** (`is_bot`/`is_deleted`/`is_admin` booleans a declarative `field_map` can carry — added to the vendored fact contract + `slack.v1.json` users.list + the `slack_user` item schema; or `status` for a normalizer like Entra's) and the **promote RPC** maps them to `account_kind`/`account_status`. An unreported flag yields `unknown`, never a defaulted `human`/`active`.

**Verification.** `saas_evidence_write_boundary_test.sql` W0–W12; **mutation-tested with 12 deliberate defects, all 12 caught** (broadened grant, direct table write, dropped tenant/provider scope, un-cleared `stale_since`, stale-after-incomplete, cross-resource completeness, removed fact-type allowlist, removed key allowlist, an `identity_accounts` write, removed audit trigger). Two were caught **only after tightening a test**: `assert raised` passed with the tenant gate deleted because two further gates also refuse cross-tenant calls — W2 now asserts *which* gate refused. A test that passes for the wrong reason is worse than no test. Runner: `saas-fact-rows.test.ts` (13). V3 tsc + full suite (2142) + eslint (0 errors) + migration-safety + RLS + manifest-validate + Next build green; runner tsc + suite green. **Harness trap, fourth time:** `test-rls.sh` re-broadens every table after migrations, masking migration revokes — `connector_run_resource_discovery` re-asserted there in lockstep. **`VENDOR.lock` `sourceSha` must be re-pinned to the v3 merge commit** once this lands; until then vendor:verify reports LOCK MISMATCH for the two changed vendored files, which is the expected visible state. **No new risk opened/closed — RISK-007 OPEN, Phase C BLOCKED, staging Supabase only, production untouched.**
